# SS14 fork feature catalog

What each fork in the ecosystem built on top of Wizard's Den, where it lives in their
tree, and — for the 154 units assessed so far — what it does to a round.
A row says a fork *has* the feature; only the shortlist says anything about taking it.

27 forks · 132 features · 803 authored · 680 vendored from another fork · 154 assessed · 17 verified takes

Portability is measured, not assumed. An independent pass re-read 68
assessments trying to refute them and corrected the graft shape of 14,
including eight of the ten features first judged self-contained. The cause is a limit of
the path data: it indexes the directories a fork *claims*, so it cannot see upstream files
a fork edits in place. Treat an unverified portability claim as a hypothesis.

● the fork authored it &nbsp;&nbsp; ○ the fork vendored it from another fork

Generated by `Tools/graft/build_markdown.py`. Edit `fork-features.yml`, not this file.

## Shortlist

11 features (17 fork implementations) of 154 assessed,
through two narrow filters. Every one was
re-read by a second agent whose job was to refute it, and nothing gets in on an
unverified claim. On top of that a feature has to be somewhere this server wants to
go, which is judgement and lives in `fit.yml` rather than being inferred.

### Take now

Verified as genuinely self-contained: the files live entirely under the fork's own namespace and nothing has to be patched upstream to make them work.

**[Server rules, space law and SOP guidebook](#guidebook-rules-and-sop) — corvax, cosmatic-drift, funky, gaby-station-now-dumont-station, goobstation, harmony-station, impstation, monolith, rmc14, serbia-strong, sunrise, viva-station**

*loop-change · drop-in · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

Opening the in-game guidebook gives players a full shelf of server-written reference material instead of just upstream's mechanical guides: numbered server rules (Cosmatic Drift's Rule 1.0 through 3.9, Harmony's R0-R8 and S1-S3), a written space/corporate law codebook listing named offences with sentences, and a Standard Operating Procedure tree split department by department and often job by job (Funky Station has separate SOP pages for Captain, HoP, Warden, Detective, Chemist, Paramedic, Roboticist, Bartender, Mime, and so on, plus a page per alert level explaining what Green through Delta actually authorise). Security and Command can look up the charge or the procedure mid-round rather than arguing from memory, and a new player can read the server's actual expectations before their first shift. Viva Station goes further with a Command-only hidden SOP: the pages exist but are metashielded, and your character only "knows" them once you physically find a head of staff's issued guidebook, with higher-clearance books unlocking more of the document, which is what gives an RP-led riot something concrete to be leaking. Sunrise and RMC-14 attach onboarding on top of the text (a guided tutorial and new-player popups), which is a much larger build than the documentation itself.

- **Take** — It is pure content in fork-namespaced XML plus guideEntry prototypes, and it is the cheapest way to make Security and Command play to a codified law and procedure instead of improvising a different standard every shift.
- **Skip** — None of the text is yours: SOP, space law and numbered rules only work if they describe your server's actual policy, so grafting is really a rewrite of every page plus permanent upkeep as your rules drift, and nothing here is mechanically enforced.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, command, service, medical, engineering, cargo, science
- **Verified** — supported. Every checkable specific held up: CD's guides.ftl lists exactly Rule 1.0-3.9 plus per-department SOP and a crimes list; Harmony's guides.ftl lists exactly R0-R8 and S1-S3; Funky's sop.yml defines per-job guideEntry pages for every job named (Captain, HoP, Warden, Detective, Chemist, Paramedic, Roboticist, Bartender, Mime) plus Green-through-Delta alert pages whose XML (GreenAlert.xml) does spell out what is authorised; Corvax's CorporateLaw dir has numbered offence chapters plus Punishments.xml/CrimeList.xml; Viva's CommandHiddenStandardOperatingProcedure.xml states verbatim the metashield and the three clearance tiers unlocked by finding specific heads' guidebooks (worth knowing: enforcement is AHelp/admin convention, not a mechanic, which the claim's "metashielded" framing correctly implies); Sunrise has _Sunrise/Tutorial and RMC-14 has _RMC14/NewPlayer, backing the onboarding aside. Drop-in is confirmed at the mechanism level: upstream GuidebookWindow.GetSortedEntries computes roots as all entries ExceptWith children, so parentless fork-namespaced guideEntry prototypes (like Funky's SOP101) surface as top-level guidebook roots with zero upstream edits. One small practical trim: Funky's sop.yml references an OctarineAlert page and alert-level-octarine locale key from its cosmic-cult port, and Violet/Octarine alert levels are fork-specific, so a graft drops or rewrites those child entries — content pruning, not entanglement.

**[Player-authored documents and books](#player-authored-text) — corvax**

*activity · drop-in · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

A document printer sits in offices as a paper-fed machine: you feed it blank paper, slot your ID card into it, and pick from a menu of about sixty ready-made Nanotrasen forms — situation reports, employment and dismissal statements, access and weapon-carry permits, search warrants, death and marriage certificates, indictments and sentences, euthanasia and shuttle requests. The printed sheet comes out already filled in with your name, your job title, the station name and the current station time, so you only write the actual content and stamp it. It also ships department stamps for the Internal Affairs Agent and the Magistrate, a legal-department pen, and a shelf of in-universe lore books players can read. Emagging the printer unlocks a handful of joke/hostile forms like liquidation notices and a declaration of military action.

- **Take** — It turns "write a form on blank paper" roleplay into a one-click, pre-filled bureaucratic workflow, giving lawyers, IAAs, command and clerks a real prop library instead of freehand typing.
- **Skip** — Every one of the ~60 form bodies and all the lore books exist only as Russian locale strings — en-US has just the four substitution tags — so grafting it means writing sixty documents of English bureaucratic prose yourself and then maintaining them.
- **Touches** — command, security, service, all-players
- **Verified** — supported; assessed as needs upstream edits, corrected by the verifier. Every gameplay specific checks out: printer.yml has MaterialStorage(Document tag)+ItemSlots(IdCard), DocumentPrinterSystem.cs substitutes doc-var-date/station/name/job exactly as described, CorvaxDocumentsPack has 59 recipes (matches "about sixty") spanning reports/certificates/permits/indictments named as claimed, CorvaxEmagDocumentsPack has 5 joke recipes including NoticeOfLiquidation and NoteBeginningMilitaryActions, rubber_stamp.yml has RubberStampIAA and RubberStampMagistrate, pen.yml has PenLegal, books.yml has a dozen+ BookLoreBase lore entries. The skip_because claim is exactly confirmed: en-US/corvax/paper/ contains only doc-printer-tags.ftl (4 tag lines), while ru-RU/corvax/paper/ has the real doc-printer.ftl, books-lore.ftl, book-busido.ftl, book-rulebook.ftl content files. Where the claim is shakier is graft_shape: every file I found (Content.Shared/Corvax/Documents/DocumentPrinterComponent.cs, Content.Server/Corvax/Documents/DocumentPrinterSystem.cs, all the Resources/Prototypes/Corvax/* yml, Resources/Locale/*/corvax/paper/*) sits inside Corvax-namespaced paths with zero upstream-directory files touched, and I found no evidence that wiring it into another codebase requires editing any upstream file (it just references generic BaseLathe/Paper/IdCard/ItemSlots APIs). The claim's own justification for "needs-upstream-edits" is a translation/content-authoring burden (60 documents untranslated), not a code-integration dependency on upstream files, so it conflates two different senses of "graft cost." Structurally this reads as drop-in with a large content-completeness caveat, not as needs-upstream-edits in the taxonomy's own terms.

2 forks built this; the assessment above is the best-verified one.

| corvax | 59 | drop-in | supported | clean |
| forky | 56 | needs upstream edits | supported | clean |

**[Tape recorder and cassettes](#tape-recorder) — cosmatic-drift, deltav, goobstation, rmc14, starlight, sunrise**

*activity · drop-in · high confidence · 5 forks carry it*

A pocket-sized tape recorder that you load with a cassette and switch between Record, Playback, Rewind and Pause from a small on-item menu. While recording it picks up anything spoken within a few tiles and writes it to the tape (about two minutes per side), timestamped with the speaker's name; voices it cannot identify come back as "Unknown" or "Unintelligible", and damaged stretches play back as garbled fragments. Hit Playback and the recorder speaks the conversation back out loud to everyone nearby, or hit "Print record transcript" and it spits out a paper transcript you can hand to security, post in the bar, or wave at the captain. Tapes can be ejected and swapped (though not while it's running), and a tape that gets unspooled has to be wound back in with a pen or screwdriver before it works again. RMC-14's take splits this into a speech recorder that prints reports plus a separate cassette player that plays music tracks.

- **Take** — It turns spoken roleplay into a physical, stealable, forgeable object, giving security real evidence, journalists real quotes and traitors a real reason to smash a tape.
- **Skip** — It quietly records everything nearby, so you inherit the moderation and consent headaches of persistent speech logs plus the ongoing cost of keeping the listener hooks working through upstream speech/radio refactors.
- **Touches** — security, service, command, all-players
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as needs upstream edits, corrected by the verifier. Fetched the actual C# sources (Content.Server/_CD/TapeRecorder/TapeRecorderSystem.cs, Content.Shared/_CD/TapeRecorder/Components/*.cs) behind the cited YAML/FTL evidence: every file lives under the fork's own _CD namespace, and the system only SubscribeLocalEvent's on ListenEvent, plus consumes ActiveListenerComponent, VoiceOverrideComponent, ChatSystem and PaperSystem — all of which I confirmed exist as stock upstream (space-wizards/space-station-14) types, not fork additions. No upstream file is patched by the fork's own copy, and nothing in the code requires editing an upstream file to wire it up (it just needs those already-standard hooks to exist, which any SS14-derived fork has). That contradicts the claim's downgrade to needs-upstream-edits; the seed's drop-in read holds up better against the evidence.
  - unsupported: "about two minutes per side" — TapeCassetteComponent.cs sets MaxCapacity = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(120) as a single continuous capacity with one CurrentPosition float and one RecordedData/Buffer list; there is no A/B-side mechanic (no flip action, no second buffer) in the code. The "on either side" phrasing is flavor text on the entity description only, not a coded two-sides system, so "two minutes per side" overstates total capacity (it reads as up to ~4 minutes, when the actual cap is 2 minutes total).

### Worth taking with a known cost

The gameplay claim held up under verification, but grafting it means editing upstream files, which is rebase tax on every future sync.

**[PDA chat messenger](#nanochat-messenger) — funky**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every crewmember's PDA becomes a phone: you get your own number, look people up by name or number, and hold private one-to-one text threads that show whether a message actually got delivered, with per-chat mute and the option to hide your number from the directory. Funky's layer on top adds named group chats — you create one, invite people by searching the crew directory, view the member list, promote others to admin, kick people out or leave yourself, with the creator marked as owner. Messages can carry picture emoji picked from a searchable, categorised picker (meme faces, character emotes) that render inline in the conversation rather than as text.

- **Take** — Gives the whole crew a private, non-radio communication channel with group coordination, which changes how departments organise without touching any combat or engineering balance.
- **Skip** — Funky's group-chat and emoji work is stitched into the vendored Delta-V NanoChat cartridge files with inline "Funky Station - Group Chats" markers rather than living in fork-only files, so you must first port the whole Delta-V messenger and then re-resolve those same shared/server/UI files on every future sync; it also hands antagonists an untraceable private group channel outside radio.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, service, antagonist
- **Needs** — [pda-cartridge-programs](#pda-cartridge-programs)
- **Verified** — supported. Every gameplay specific maps to a fetched file: DeliveryFailed, MutedChats, ListNumber, group-chat enum members and Owner/Admin fields all carrying inline "Funky Station - Group Chats" markers inside vendored _DV files, plus categorised/searchable emoji prototypes rendered inline by an EmojiTag markup handler. The needs-upstream-edits downgrade is correct in both senses: code search shows the Funky markers live in four _DV files (not _Funkystation), and the fork's copy of the true upstream Resources/Prototypes/Entities/Objects/Devices/pda.yml is edited in place to add NanoChatCartridge to crew PDA program lists.

3 forks built this; the assessment above is the best-verified one.

**[Supermatter engine](#supermatter-engine) — serbia-strong**

*structural · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 5 forks carry it*

Engineering gets a giant crystal as an alternative station power source, and babysitting it is a full-time job. The crystal ships inert; hitting it with an object or a shot wakes it up, after which it eats the gas around it, throws lightning arcs into tesla coils and radiation into collectors, and belches out superheated oxygen and plasma that has to be cooled and filtered by a chamber the engineers build themselves (no map ships a ready-made one). Engineers feed it matter and energy with dedicated supermatter emitters they tune by hand, and watch its integrity, internal energy, matter, temperature and per-gas effects on a monitoring console or on a PDA app anyone can install. Standing near it without meson goggles and rad protection gives you hallucinations and radiation sickness. Let its energy drift outside the safe band and integrity bleeds away: engineering radio warns first, then the whole station is told, then Central Command hears the delamination countdown, and if nobody drags it back in time the crystal explodes, or turns into a singularity or a tesla ball that starts eating the station.

- **Take** — It is the single richest engineering minigame in SS13 that upstream SS14 lacks: a permanent, multi-person job for the engineering department whose failure state is a station-wide catastrophe the whole crew has to react to.
- **Skip** — It is a ~100-file simulation (thermodynamics, per-gas response curves, integrity, delamination variants, its own emitter, console, cartridge, hardsuit and audio) that only pays off if you also commit to map work, containment-chamber design and balance tuning; a badly ported version either never delaminates or delaminates every round.
- **Touches** — engineering, atmospherics, command, all-players
- **Verified** — supported. Directly cloned the fork's tree (git ls-tree) and confirmed all ~121 SuperMatter/SuperMatterEmitter/Observer/Cartridge .cs and .yml files live under SS220/ namespace dirs, matching the drop-in path-data signal. But content-grepping non-namespace files (something path data cannot do) found the fork editing three genuinely upstream files in place: Resources/Prototypes/Guidebook/engineering.yml (adds \"- Supermatter # SS220-superMatter-crystal\" to the vanilla guide-category list), Resources/Prototypes/Research/industrial.yml, and Resources/Prototypes/Recipes/Lathes/Packs/engineering.yml (both add \"SuperMatterObserverCircuitboard # SS220 superMatter-crystal\" to vanilla tech-unlock/lathe-recipe lists). These are real edits to upstream files carrying SS220 comment tags, not new files, so this is fork-patches-upstream, not merely integration-would-require-upstream-edits. The gameplay description itself checks out almost line-for-line against the Russian guidebook text and the crystal.yml/UI-state code (inert-until-hit activation, gas consumption, LightningArcShooter/RadiationSource output to tesla coils/collectors, hot O2+plasma byproduct with \"NT currently has no standard containment chamber\" text matching \"no map ships a ready-made one,\" SuperMatterObserverUpdateState exposing integrity/energy/matter/temperature/per-gas ratios, freely-installable Cartridge-type PDA app, and the three-stage engineering-radio to station-wide to CentCom delamination warning sequence verbatim in the guidebook, ending in Explosive/Singularity/TeslaEnergyBall outcomes).
  - unsupported: "Standing near it without meson goggles and rad protection gives you hallucinations and radiation sickness" — the guidebook and crystal.yml only show a generic `EyeProtection` component check (satisfied by any eye-protection item, e.g. "engineering goggles") plus a bare `RadiationSource`; no file names a specific "meson goggles" or "rad protection" item, so the general mechanic is right but the specific gear names are the claim author's gloss, not something the evidence states.

2 forks built this; the assessment above is the best-verified one.

**[Reagent and reaction expansion](#reagent-and-reaction-expansion) — sunrise**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 13 forks carry it*

Chemists get a handful of new drugs to mix and a new way to hand them out: a hemostatic powder that clamps bleeding fast but poisons you past ten units, an anti-radiation drug that leaves the patient seeing rainbows, and a pressure-proofing stimulant that makes you jitter, stutter, scream and occasionally vomit while it keeps you alive in vacuum. The chem master can now print labelled medicated patches alongside pills and bottles, so medics can slap a dose on someone instead of injecting. Bartenders also get a run of real cocktails (Negroni, Old Fashioned, Mai Tai, Paloma, Dark and Stormy, Boyarsky, Yorsh and others) with their own flavour text, plus extra sodas and condiments.

- **Take** — The patch printer is a genuinely new delivery format for chemistry, and the cocktail list is free bartender depth that costs nothing but YAML.
- **Skip** — The medical reagents are written against Sunrise's custom Mangleness damage type and their status-effect prototypes, so they will not compile or balance sensibly without dragging in the fork's wound model; several of the listed recipe files are empty placeholders, so the headline file count overstates what is actually there.
- **Touches** — medical, science, service, all-players
- **Needs** — [surgery-and-limb-wounds](#surgery-and-limb-wounds)
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched all 10 cited files plus the base (non-_Sunrise) damage-type files. Every headline specific in the claim checks out verbatim in Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Chemistry/medicine.yml: HemostaticPowder does ModifyBleed -4.2 and adds Poison damage only once a ReagentCondition min:10.1 is met ("poisons past ten units"); Retroindralyne heals Radiation -3 and triggers StatusEffectSeeingRainbow ("seeing rainbows"); BarozinePlus grants a PressureImmunity GenericStatusEffect plus Jitter, a Stutter component, a Scream Emote, and probability-gated Vomit ("jitter, stutter, scream and occasionally vomit... keeps you alive in vacuum"). Content.Server/_Sunrise/Chemistry/EntitySystems/ChemMasterSystem.Patches.cs literally implements OnCreatePatchesMessage, spawning and labelling "Patch" entities from the chem master — confirms patch printing. Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Reagents/Consumable/Drink/alcohol.yml contains Boyarsky, DarkAndStormy, MaiTai, Negroni, OldFashioned, Paloma, Yorsh — every cocktail named in the claim is present. The three "empty placeholder" files (Recipes/narcotics.yml, Recipes/fun.yml, Reagents/fun.yml) really do return HTTP 200 with zero bytes, exactly matching the skip_because note about inflated file counts. On graft_shape: the seed's drop-in guess is wrong and the assessor's needs-upstream-edits downgrade is not just correct but understates how entangled it is. I traced the Mangleness damage type used by HemostaticPowder/Retroindralyne to Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Damage/types.yml, and found the fork's own (non-_Sunrise) Resources/Prototypes/Damage/containers.yml has a "Mangleness # Sunrise-Edit" line spliced into an existing damageContainer — an in-place edit of an upstream-owned file, invisible to path-based scoping. Separately, the patch-printing feature required editing three more upstream files in place: Content.Server/Chemistry/EntitySystems/ChemMasterSystem.cs (marked partial, new SubscribeLocalEvent for ChemMasterCreatePatchesMessage, extra UI-state argument), and Content.Shared/Chemistry/SharedChemMaster.cs (new ChemMasterCreatePatchesMessage class, new PatchDosageLimit field on the existing UI state), all tagged "Sunrise-Edit start/end". This is direct proof of fork-patches-upstream (the fork's own tree already touches non-namespaced upstream files), and by the same token any integration into a different codebase would need equivalent edits to that codebase's damage-container and ChemMaster files, so integration-would-require-upstream-edits also holds.
  - unsupported: "so medics can slap a dose on someone instead of injecting" is a plausible but unverified inference — I confirmed the patch-printing UI/server flow (OnCreatePatchesMessage, labelling, dosage) but did not fetch the Patch entity prototype or its interaction code to confirm topical/touch application vs. some other consumption mechanic.

**[Objective and uplink store rework](#antag-objectives-and-uplink-rework) — starlight**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Traitors get a set of open-ended "story" objectives aimed at a randomly chosen department instead of only steal/kill tasks: frame someone in Engineering as a Syndicate agent, spread rumours that Medical is starting an uprising, publicly disgrace a department head, impersonate a Cargo tech and trash their reputation, stir up a feud between two departments, or waste a department's resources however you like. Because nothing checks those automatically, an antagonist gets a "Write objective summary" button and a text window they can edit all shift to explain how they pulled it off; whatever they wrote is printed under their name in the round-end report. The uplink also grows a "Second Hand" tab that stocks 8-14 random cut-price used Syndicate items per round (one each, then the entry vanishes), plus limited-stock listings, listings that only appear for certain roles, at certain player counts, or when you rolled a specific objective (for example a whole Die-A-Glorious-Death aisle of grenade rigs, ammo duffels and Antimov boards that only unlocks if you drew the suicide-mission objective), and extra aisles for allied cyborgs, cybernetics, implants and joke purchases.

- **Take** — The story objectives plus the player-written round-end summary turn traitoring from a checklist into social play, and they are the rare antag content that makes the whole crew's shift more interesting rather than just arming one player harder.
- **Skip** — Unverifiable objectives lean entirely on player honesty and admin attention, and the bundle drags in a pile of antag-specific conditions (abductor, absorb, blood drain, damn, brighteye) that are dead weight unless you already run those antags.
- **Touches** — antagonist, traitor, all-players, command, security
- **Needs** — [abductors](#abductors), [changeling](#changeling), [vampire](#vampire), [devil-pacts](#devil-pacts)
- **Verified** — supported. Cloned the fork and traced SecondHandSystem's `StoreInitializedEvent` back to its definition in Content.Server/StoreDiscount/Systems/StoreDiscountSystem.cs — a file OUTSIDE any _Starlight namespace dir (not in evidence_cited, invisible to the path-data seed). Diffing against space-wizards/space-station-14 master's identical-path file proves the fork added a `UseSecondHand` field to the record struct and a `using Content.Server._Starlight.StoreDiscount;` import that upstream's version does not have — a real in-place edit of an upstream file, not just a same-fork dependency. This falsifies the drop-in seed on its own strict terms (files outside fork namespace exist and were edited) independent of the claim's own (weaker) reasoning about changeling/vampire/abductor/devil-pact files, which I also confirmed exist as genuine siblings in the same Objectives folder/namespace (self-contained, not themselves upstream edits).
  - unsupported: "extra aisles for allied cyborgs, cybernetics, implants and joke purchases" implies these are general traitor-uplink additions, but allies.yml shows most cyborg-ally listings (kitted/speed borgi, all four cyborg reinforcement radios, both mech-teleporter crates) are gated behind `StoreWhitelistCondition` requiring `NukeOpsUplink` or `LoneOpsUplink` tags — only one basic borgi-kit listing is unrestricted for a general traitor uplink; the touches list also omits nukeops/lone-ops entirely, so framing this as a traitor-facing aisle overstates general availability.
  - unsupported: "8-14 random cut-price used Syndicate items" — the code confirms the 8-14 count and one-each/vanish-on-purchase mechanic precisely (SecondHandSystem.cs), but no discount/cost-reduction field was found on SecondHandCategoryPrototype or in the SecondHand wiring itself; 'cut-price' is a reasonable inference (there's a companion duffelbag_secondhand.yml and the sibling StoreDiscount system already does price cuts) but isn't directly confirmed in the files inspected.

**[Exotic gases and reactions](#exotic-gas-expansion) — forky**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Atmospherics gets a much larger chemistry set: alongside the usual air, the station can now brew BZ, healium, nitrium, pluoxium, hyper-noblium, proto-nitrate, zauker, halon, helium and hydrogen by mixing existing gases at the right temperatures and pressures, and each one does something distinct when someone breathes it. Healium heals burns, brute and toxins and eventually puts the patient to sleep, so a flooded room can be a field hospital; BZ makes people see rainbows, cough and stumble around at two-thirds speed; nitrium is a stimulant that gets stronger the more you inhale; halon smothers fires by eating the oxygen and chilling the room; zauker is straight poison. Air alarms learn to warn about the new gases, an electrolyzer machine burns plasma or uranium sheets to split water vapour into hydrogen and oxygen (and convert hyper-noblium or BZ into other exotics), and freezing hydrogen with BZ compresses it into metal hydrogen sheets that engineers craft into a lightweight prying fire axe and a set of armour and helmet.

- **Take** — It turns atmospherics from a life-support chore into a production department with real outputs other roles want: healing gas for medical, firefighting gas for engineering, stimulants, and a craftable metal hydrogen tool and armour set at the end of the chain.
- **Skip** — Ten new gases mean every upstream gas reaction prototype, air alarm default, atmos UI readout and the shared gas enum have to grow with them, and the reagent effects lean on fork-specific reagents and status effects, so this is a permanent merge-conflict surface on files upstream keeps touching.
- **Touches** — engineering, atmospherics, medical, science, salvage, all-players
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched the cited YAML/locale/C# files and every specific claim checked out: gases.yml defines BZ (rainbow emote, cough, sleep via ForcedSleeping status), Healium (large Burn/Toxin/Brute heals plus sleep), Nitrium (tiered speed-boost by dose, matching "stronger the more you inhale"), Zauker (Slash/Heat/Poison damage-over-time, i.e. poison), reactions.yml defines gasReaction prototypes producing BZ, Healium, Nitrium, Pluoxium, HyperNoblium, Halon and a MetalHydrogenReaction gated on BZ+Hydrogen at low temperature; airalarms.yml adds threshold prototypes for the new gases; ElectrolyzerSystem.cs literally burns Plasma/Uranium sheet tags to convert WaterVapor into Hydrogen+Oxygen and separately consumes HyperNoblium/BZ; h2_fireaxe.yml is a Prying-tool fire axe described as "a lightweight crowbar ... fire axe head attached"; h2_armor.yml defines MetalHydrogenArmor/Helmet construction recipes. Going beyond the cited evidence, I fetched Content.Shared/Atmos/Atmospherics.cs (an upstream file, not under any _Funkystation directory) and found it directly edited: the shared `Gas` enum was extended from a smaller upstream set to include BZ=9 through AntiNoblium=19, `TotalNumberOfGases` was bumped to 20, and roughly a dozen molar/specific-heat constants carry "// Funky Atmos - /tg/ gases" comments marking in-place additions. This is stronger than the claim's own skip_because reasoning ("the shared gas enum have to grow") predicted — it isn't hypothetical, the fork's live copy already patches that upstream file directly, contradicting the drop-in seed outright.

**[Access control rework](#access-control-rework) — serbia-strong**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Some doors only open if you approach them from the right side, so a checkpoint airlock lets people out freely but refuses anyone pushing in from the wrong direction, and certain doors can be gated on what you *are* rather than what your ID says, letting a door open for a borg or a specific faction while rejecting a stolen card. Certain items refuse to be picked up, worn or dragged by the wrong person at all: grab a weapon or piece of gear you're not cleared for and it slips out of your hands with a warning message, a buzz, and in some cases a shock that hurts you for trying. There is also a painter machine with a slot for a PDA and a slot for an ID card that reskins them to look like a different department's, so you can walk around presenting as someone you aren't, and the ID access list itself is expanded with extra levels like Psychologist, Interdyne and USSP for fork-specific factions and jobs.

- **Take** — Item-level ID restriction is the piece upstream genuinely lacks: it stops gear theft at the point of pickup instead of relying on players to respect job boundaries, and it gives security a real reason that a stolen ID is not a master key.
- **Skip** — The pieces are not self-contained: directional access has to be consulted from upstream's access reader, and the whitelist door check listens for a fork-only door event that upstream door code must be patched to raise, so grafting it means carrying permanent edits in upstream files that will conflict on every sync. The access-level prototypes are also tied to SS220's own factions (Interdyne, USSP) and are dead weight without them.
- **Touches** — security, command, medical, engineering, service, antagonist, all-players
- **Verified** — supported. I cloned the fork and grepped it directly. Content.Shared/Doors/Systems/SharedDoorSystem.cs (an upstream file, not under any SS220 namespace) is patched in place inside HasAccess(): it raises a fork-only DoorAccesAttemptEvent (consumed by Content.Shared/SS220/AccessWhitelist/SharedAccessWhitelistSystem.cs) and calls the fork's DirectionalAccessSystem.IsDirectionAllowed, both wrapped in '// SS220-directional-access' / '//SS220 ChameleonStructure' comment markers. That is exactly the 'listens for a fork-only door event that upstream door code must be patched to raise' claim in skip_because, and it is a real in-place edit to an upstream file, not just a hypothetical integration cost. The item-restriction (SharedRestrictedItemSystem.cs: cancels BeingEquippedAttemptEvent/BeingPulledAttemptEvent/GettingPickedUpAttemptEvent, popup + damage + sound on fail), the PDA/ID painter (PdaIdPainterComponent with IdCardSlot/PdaSlot and Chosen/Default EntProtoId fields, self-contained), and the extra access levels (Psychologist in medical.yml, Interdyne in interdyne.yml + access.ftl, USSP in ussp.yml) all read exactly as described.

2 forks built this; the assessment above is the best-verified one.

| Fork | Files | Graft shape | Verified | Checked claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| serbia-strong | 130 | needs upstream edits | supported | clean |
| sunrise | 43 | needs upstream edits | supported | 2 overclaim(s) |

**[Melee weapons, shields and blocking](#melee-weapons-and-blocking) — serbia-strong**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 5 forks carry it*

Shields stop being passive luck and become something you actively hold up: a dedicated keybind raises the shield, an "Active Blocking" indicator appears on your HUD, your character visibly changes stance, and your chance to stop incoming melee and gunfire jumps (a riot shield goes from roughly a coin-flip to blocking about three attacks in four), at the cost of not being able to throw anything while braced. Shields now have their own condition you can examine and they wear down under fire, eventually shattering into steel, glass, planks or webbing depending on what they were made of, and there is a full ladder of them: cardboard, wooden buckler, makeshift steel, web, riot, laser-dissipating, heavy ballistic (which slows you down), collapsible telescopic, mirror, clockwork, and toggleable energy shields including a syndicate red variant. Armour and shields also work by threshold rather than percentage, so a weak hit bounces off entirely while a heavy one punches through, holding a raised shield resists the instant stun from batons, and some armour converts part of a stunbaton's stamina hit into shock damage instead of dropping you outright.

- **Take** — It turns melee and close-range fights from a stat-check into an active exchange, and specifically defangs the stunbaton-into-cuffs instant-win that dominates security versus antagonist encounters.
- **Skip** — It replaces the whole armour and stun pipeline, not just shields: threshold armour, stamina conversion and instastun resistance rebalance every existing weapon, armour set and antagonist kit at once, and it needs a new keybind wired into upstream input handling, so you inherit a permanent balance-tuning job.
- **Touches** — security, antagonist, all-players, cargo
- **Verified** — supported. I found direct edits to three genuinely-upstream, non-SS220 files: Content.Shared/Input/ContentKeyFunctions.cs (a vanilla file listing dozens of engine key functions) has 'ToggleActiveBlocking' spliced in at line 11 among stock entries like Drop/ExamineEntity/FocusChat; Resources/keybinds.yml has a block literally fenced '#SS220 shield rework begin' binding ToggleActiveBlocking to key B; and Content.Client/Input/ContentContexts.cs adds 'human.AddFunction(ContentKeyFunctions.ToggleActiveBlocking); //SS220 shield rework'. That settles needs-upstream-edits as fork-patches-upstream, not just integration-would-require-upstream-edits. The gameplay claim itself checks out in fine detail: shields.yml shows 11 named shield prototypes (Riot, RiotLaser, RiotBullet, WoodenBuckler, Card/cardboard, Makeshift/steel, Web, Clockwork, Mirror, Energy, Telescopic) each with AltArmor tresholdDict/durabilityTresholdDict (threshold not percentage) and Destructible behaviors spawning SheetSteel/SheetGlass, MaterialWoodPlank, or MaterialWebSilk on break; RiotBulletShieldAlt has ClothingSpeedModifier/HeldSpeedModifier 0.9 (the slow-you-down heavy ballistic shield); EnergyShieldAlt is ItemToggle-based and syndi.yml adds a red-lit EnergyShieldSyndi variant; BaseShieldAlt carries InstastunResist/InstastunResistOnActiveBlocking (baton-stun resistance while blocking); StaminaConvertArmorComponent.cs literally converts a DamageCoefficient of stamina damage into a configured DamageType (e.g. Shock); blocking.yml is the 'Active Blocking' HUD alert; and SharedAltBlockingSystem.User.cs wires the toggle to ContentKeyFunctions.ToggleActiveBlocking and cancels ThrowAttemptEvent while blocking (can't throw while braced). Only minor imprecisions found, noted in overclaims.
  - unsupported: "your chance to stop incoming melee and gunfire jumps (a riot shield goes from roughly a coin-flip to blocking about three attacks in four)" blends two different stats: RiotShieldAlt's actual AltBlocking values are rangeBlockProb 0.50 -> activeRangeBlockProb 0.60 (ranged/gunfire), and meleeBlockProb 0.65 -> activeMeleeBlockProb 0.75 (melee). No single stat goes from 0.50 to 0.75; the claim cherry-picks the passive number from one axis (ranged) and the active number from the other (melee) to build a punchier 'coin-flip to 3-in-4' line.
  - unsupported: "there is a full ladder of them" implies an exhaustive list, but the fork also ships a Dagon's Scale cult shield (Resources/Prototypes/SS220/Entities/Objects/Shields/dagots_scale.yml, parent BaseShieldAlt) that isn't in the enumerated ladder.

**[Long-running medical conditions](#medical-status-conditions) — serbia-strong**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Taking recreational drugs leaves traces in your blood for about ten minutes after the last dose, and anyone holding a narcotics test swab can grab a suspect, spend a few seconds swabbing them, and then read the result off the used swab: positive or negative, with the subject's DNA left on it as evidence. Separately, the body remembers every chemical it has processed, keeping a per-drug tolerance counter that builds with repeated doses and fades on its own after a while, so a character who keeps hitting the same drug is no longer the same patient as one taking it for the first time. The tolerance counter is bookkeeping only in the files here; the actual "the drug does less for you now" part lives in whichever chemical effects choose to read it.

- **Take** — It turns drug use into something with consequences a second player can act on: security gets a real, DNA-tagged drug test instead of guessing, and repeat users build a tolerance the round tracks.
- **Skip** — The interesting half is missing from these files: tolerance is a counter nobody in this set consumes, and the "you used a narcotic" signal has to be raised from chemistry code that isn't fork-namespaced, so you inherit an upstream patch plus SS220's CLA-licensed source with a hosting restriction that will not sit comfortably next to AGPL fork code.
- **Touches** — medical, security, all-players
- **Verified** — supported. Cloned the repo and grepped for the actual wiring: Content.Shared/Metabolism/MetabolizerSystem.cs (upstream namespace/path, no SS220 in it) is patched in place with "// ss220 add narcotics test start/end" comments that raise MetabolizeNarcoticEvent when a reagent's Group == NarcoticsGroup — this is the only place the event is raised. Likewise Content.Shared/EntityEffects/Effects/StatusEffects/MovementSpeedModifierEntityEffectSystem.cs (also upstream path) is patched with "SS220 add movement speed modification depending on the adaptation" to call ChemicalAdaptationSystem.TryGetMetabolized. Both are genuine upstream-directory files carrying fork edits, which directly falsifies the seed's drop-in/zero-files-outside-namespace path-data guess and confirms the claim's own "needs-upstream-edits" call, right down to the specific mechanism it named (narcotic signal raised from non-fork-namespaced chemistry code).

### Ruled out by direction

These passed verification and were still turned down. Kept visible with the reason,
because "we considered that and said no" is what gets lost and re-proposed later.

- [Round modifiers and game director](#round-modifier-presets) — A game director is a game master by another name, so the same call applies.
- [Department restructure](#department-restructure) — Not the direction we are working towards.
- [Ghost roles, respawn and game-master roles](#gamemaster-and-ghost-roles) — Anything with a game master is too much.
- [Games, instruments and performance](#games-instruments-and-performance) — Not really interesting.
- [Extra playable species](#extra-playable-species) — Too much.

## Features

## Antag

### Objective and uplink store rework

<a id="antag-objectives-and-uplink-rework"></a>

Extends or replaces the antagonist objective framework and the uplink storefront with new conditions, currencies, catalog categories and round-end summaries.

**rmc14**

*structural · entangled · high confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Instead of personal antag objectives, the whole marine side works one shared intelligence economy. Players scavenge the colony for paper scraps, progress reports, folders and technical manuals, read them in order (you can't understand a manual until you've read simpler documents), type what they learned into the intel computer, then store the document back in the intel room. Other point sources include hauling out experimental devices, decrypting data disks at a SIGINT terminal using a password found in earlier clues, hacking planetside data terminals (which need colony power and comms restored first), cracking safes whose combinations come from document clues, rescuing survivors back to the ship, morgue-ing dead colonists, and dragging dead xenos into the research containment room (bigger corpse, more points). A handheld detector pings the direction of nearby intel, a shared "View Intel Objectives" action shows team progress, and the points get spent at a CIC tech tree console on tiered unlocks: an armoured vehicle, extra requisitions and dropship budget, orbital bombardment warheads, waking reinforcement troops, each with a station-wide announcement when it lands.

- **Take** — It turns "objectives" from a private checklist into a visible, shared side-wide economy where exploration and corpse recovery buy real round-changing assets, giving non-combat players a concrete way to shape how the round goes.
- **Skip** — Nothing about it is portable as-is: the point sources assume RMC-14's colony/warship map layout, xeno corpses, survivors and dedicated intel jobs, and the spend side buys orbital cannon warheads, dropship and requisitions budgets and reinforcement waves that only exist in that fork, so you'd be rebuilding both ends around your own round structure.
- **Touches** — command, cargo, science, medical, security, all-players
- **Needs** — [alternate-round-types](#alternate-round-types), [extra-job-roster](#extra-job-roster), [cargo-bounties-and-market](#cargo-bounties-and-market)
- **Verified** — supported. Re-fetched both cited files plus the Content.Shared/_RMC14/Intel component directory listing and the detector system source. Every specific gameplay beat in the claim checks out against the guidebook text almost verbatim: read-order gating (scrap->report->folder->manual), entering knowledge into the intel computer then re-storing the document, SIGINT terminal disk decryption with clue-derived keys, planetside data terminals gated on colony power+comms, safe combinations from clues, survivors counted on ship arrival, colonist corpses to morgue, xeno corpses to research containment scaled by size, and a CIC tech tree spending intel points on an ARC vehicle, requisitions/dropship budget increases, orbital-cannon warheads, and waking cryo troops/specialists, each firing a TechAnnounceEvent. The detector system I read independently (not in the cited evidence) confirms a motion-detector-style blip mechanic keyed to IntelDetectorTrackedComponent (present on all intel item prototypes), matching the 'pings nearby intel' claim. On graft_shape: all files I found (Resources/Prototypes/_RMC14/..., Content.Shared/_RMC14/Intel/...) stay inside the fork's own _RMC14 namespace, so the seed's path-based 'zero files outside namespace' finding holds — but the detector system's imports (Xenonids.Parasite, motion detector, requisitions inventory) and the tech tree's direct calls into RMCOrbitalCannonWarhead*, VehicleHumveeARC, dropship/requisitions budget events, and cryo-marine waking confirm genuine functional entanglement with other RMC14-specific systems, exactly matching the claim's own 'entangled' downgrade and its listed reasons.
  - unsupported: "each with a station-wide announcement when it lands" — the TechAnnounceEvent fires at the moment the tech-tree purchase is made, not tied specifically to a delivery/landing event; every tier purchase (including non-delivery ones like tier unlocks) gets the same announcement, so 'when it lands' slightly over-specifies the trigger.

**starlight**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Traitors get a set of open-ended "story" objectives aimed at a randomly chosen department instead of only steal/kill tasks: frame someone in Engineering as a Syndicate agent, spread rumours that Medical is starting an uprising, publicly disgrace a department head, impersonate a Cargo tech and trash their reputation, stir up a feud between two departments, or waste a department's resources however you like. Because nothing checks those automatically, an antagonist gets a "Write objective summary" button and a text window they can edit all shift to explain how they pulled it off; whatever they wrote is printed under their name in the round-end report. The uplink also grows a "Second Hand" tab that stocks 8-14 random cut-price used Syndicate items per round (one each, then the entry vanishes), plus limited-stock listings, listings that only appear for certain roles, at certain player counts, or when you rolled a specific objective (for example a whole Die-A-Glorious-Death aisle of grenade rigs, ammo duffels and Antimov boards that only unlocks if you drew the suicide-mission objective), and extra aisles for allied cyborgs, cybernetics, implants and joke purchases.

- **Take** — The story objectives plus the player-written round-end summary turn traitoring from a checklist into social play, and they are the rare antag content that makes the whole crew's shift more interesting rather than just arming one player harder.
- **Skip** — Unverifiable objectives lean entirely on player honesty and admin attention, and the bundle drags in a pile of antag-specific conditions (abductor, absorb, blood drain, damn, brighteye) that are dead weight unless you already run those antags.
- **Touches** — antagonist, traitor, all-players, command, security
- **Needs** — [abductors](#abductors), [changeling](#changeling), [vampire](#vampire), [devil-pacts](#devil-pacts)
- **Verified** — supported. Cloned the fork and traced SecondHandSystem's `StoreInitializedEvent` back to its definition in Content.Server/StoreDiscount/Systems/StoreDiscountSystem.cs — a file OUTSIDE any _Starlight namespace dir (not in evidence_cited, invisible to the path-data seed). Diffing against space-wizards/space-station-14 master's identical-path file proves the fork added a `UseSecondHand` field to the record struct and a `using Content.Server._Starlight.StoreDiscount;` import that upstream's version does not have — a real in-place edit of an upstream file, not just a same-fork dependency. This falsifies the drop-in seed on its own strict terms (files outside fork namespace exist and were edited) independent of the claim's own (weaker) reasoning about changeling/vampire/abductor/devil-pact files, which I also confirmed exist as genuine siblings in the same Objectives folder/namespace (self-contained, not themselves upstream edits).
  - unsupported: "extra aisles for allied cyborgs, cybernetics, implants and joke purchases" implies these are general traitor-uplink additions, but allies.yml shows most cyborg-ally listings (kitted/speed borgi, all four cyborg reinforcement radios, both mech-teleporter crates) are gated behind `StoreWhitelistCondition` requiring `NukeOpsUplink` or `LoneOpsUplink` tags — only one basic borgi-kit listing is unrestricted for a general traitor uplink; the touches list also omits nukeops/lone-ops entirely, so framing this as a traitor-facing aisle overstates general availability.
  - unsupported: "8-14 random cut-price used Syndicate items" — the code confirms the 8-14 count and one-each/vanish-on-purchase mechanic precisely (SecondHandSystem.cs), but no discount/cost-reduction field was found on SecondHandCategoryPrototype or in the SecondHand wiring itself; 'cut-price' is a reasonable inference (there's a companion duffelbag_secondhand.yml and the sibling StoreDiscount system already does price cuts) but isn't directly confirmed in the files inspected.

**impstation**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

A traitor opening their uplink sees a much bigger, more personalised shop: extra shelves of gear (attack animals and card packs, chem darts, bullet-catchers, zombie-plague injectors), items that only appear if you hold a specific job (hot sauce crates for the chef, shock darts for the zookeeper), items that only appear if you drew a specific objective (a suicide-mission traitor gets offered a destabilising crystal), rotating discounts, and one-off stock that has to restock before you can buy again. Security gets its own separate storefront, SecLink, spending three distinct token types on batons, disablers and utility gear: stun sabres, a pocket disabler, a citationator that fires tickets, a collapsible shield, surplus night-vision goggles and a handheld criminal-records terminal, with drained items needing a recharge before they can be refunded. Alongside that, several fork-only antagonist roles get their own objective sets, such as a Syndicate recruiter who must talk a handful of disgruntled crew into signing a contract in blood with their pen, keep that pen safe, and escape with their new hires, or a chem-dealing specialist who must peddle concoctions and survive to the shuttle.

- **Take** — It turns the uplink from a flat price list into a shop that reacts to your job and your objective, and the SecLink token store gives security a progression-flavoured loadout choice they normally never get.
- **Skip** — The catalogue entries are mostly pointers at dozens of fork-only item entities and fork-only antag roles, so grafting the storefront without those first gives you a menu full of missing prototypes; the custom client uplink window also has to displace the upstream store UI rather than sit beside it.
- **Touches** — antagonist, security, service, all-players
- **Needs** — [traitor-uplink](#traitor-uplink), [minor-antag-roles](#minor-antag-roles), [changeling](#changeling), [heretic](#heretic), [wizard-magic](#wizard-magic)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**ephemeral-space**

*structural · entangled · high confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Antagonists no longer get a private shopping list of "steal X, kill Y" tasks. Instead the whole traitor side shares one visible chain: a handful of smaller jobs first (kill a specific crewmember, bug a department's APC, sabotage the comms computer, crew monitoring server, criminal records, emergency access terminal or telecom processor), and only once those are done can they hack three Security Override Terminals scattered in the HoP's secure room, the captain's office and secure tech storage to unlock the nuclear code, then arm the nuke with the captain's disk. Each objective shows in a panel tagged Team or Solo with a live percentage of how far along it is, and at round end each line reads Success or Failed with that percentage. Kill targets can be handed to you under a codename rather than a name, and an ability lets you sense roughly how far away and in which direction your target is. The crew have their own counterpart objective (keep the station powered, outrun the radstorm and charge the warp drive to escape), and a sympathizer variant simply asks a hidden helper to work out who the traitors are and help them set off the nuke.

- **Take** — It turns antag objectives from a private end-of-round scorecard into the round's actual spine: a visible, prerequisite-gated team chain with live progress percentages that both sides are effectively racing over, which is a far stronger driver of round narrative than upstream's independent steal/kill list.
- **Skip** — It is not an objective library you can bolt on, it is Ephemeral Space's whole round: the objectives assume secret identities and organisations to assign them, a warp drive and radstorm for the crew side, fork-only consoles (emergency access terminal, radio scrambler, crypto nuke terminals) as sabotage targets, and a three-terminal nuke flow that replaces the standard nuke round. Grafting it means importing those systems or rewriting every condition against your own station's machines.
- **Touches** — antagonist, all-players, command, security, engineering, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [secret-identities](#secret-identities), [warp-drive](#warp-drive), [alternate-round-types](#alternate-round-types)
- **Verified** — supported. Every specific gameplay beat checks out against the raw files: objective.ftl has the Team/Solo tags and "(N%)" / Success-Failed summary line verbatim; ESTargetCodenameComponent and ESSenseTargetDistanceSystem confirm codenamed kill targets and the directional-sense popup with adjacent/close/moderate/far tiers; traitor.yml lists exactly the minor sabotage set (comms, crew monitoring, criminal records, emergency access terminal, telecom processor, bug an APC) gated by ESNukePrereqObjective before ESObjectiveTraitorHackCryptoNukeConsoles ("HoP's secure room, the captain's office, and secure tech storage"), then ESObjectiveTraitorDetonateNuke with the captain's disk; crew.yml's ESObjectiveWarpDrive description matches the "outrun the radstorm/charge the warp drive" line word for word; ESObjectiveSympathizerDetonateNuke matches the sympathizer description. The claim's own downgrade from the seed's "drop-in" to "entangled" is itself correct: repo-tree search shows ESSecretIdentityRole is a hook into a large separate SecretIdentity system (Arsonist/Avenger/Burstworm/Cryojunkie sub-roles), ESWarpDriveObjective hooks a full separate WarpDrive gamerule/singularity system, and Resources/Prototypes/_ES/GameRules/presets.yml shows the whole objective chain is wired into the fork's own always-on ESMasqueradeManaged/ESExtended presets (with ESWarpDriveRule baked in) rather than being an optional bolt-on round type. depends_on (secret-identities, warp-drive, alternate-round-types) is accurate.

**backmen**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Antagonists get a wider spread of things to be told to do and a much bigger shop to spend on. New assignments include hijacking the evacuation shuttle (allowed to leave with cuffed hostages and fellow agents alive), stealing the nuke disc as a non-command traitor, impersonating a specific crew member and holding that identity until the shuttle leaves, gaining psychic powers and keeping them, and, for a blob, spreading to a set tile count. Steal lists add fork-only prizes like an energy shotgun and a telescopic baton. The syndicate uplink stocks a lot more: magazines and ammo boxes for the fork's guns, a short-range teleport knife with four charges, a chainsword, a codespeak language manual readable twice, mime and clown disguises, and a spread of psionic, BZ and exotic-gas grenade bundles. Some antags shop in their own currency instead of telecrystals, for example the flesh cultist trading harvested mutation points for one-off body upgrades like a blade arm or immunity to shocks, pressure and cold, and the station itself gets a round-start goal drawn from a long list (build a singularity, set up a zoo, repair xenobiology, run security training).

- **Take** — It turns a thin objective pool into real variety, and the impersonate and hijack conditions in particular push traitors toward social play instead of another murder checklist.
- **Skip** — Most of the catalogue is a shopfront for content the fork already has, so grafting it without the fork's guns, psionics, blob and flesh cult leaves you with a store full of items that do not exist; and a chunk of the listing text is untranslated Russian you would have to rewrite.
- **Touches** — antagonist, security, command, all-players
- **Needs** — [traitor-uplink](#traitor-uplink), [firearms-content-expansion](#firearms-content-expansion), [psionics-and-glimmer](#psionics-and-glimmer), [blob](#blob), [station-goals](#station-goals), [language-system](#language-system)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Round modifiers and game director

<a id="round-modifier-presets"></a>

Round-level frameworks that pick which threats and rulesets are in play, from weighted secret directors to named round modes, shift-intensity tiers and rolled round-wide aspects.

**starlight**

*structural · entangled · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Instead of one fixed threat picked at roundstart, the shift runs on a hidden budget that spends itself over the hour: it buys a headline threat at the start (traitors, nukies, revs, cosmic cult, zombies, a wizard duel), sprinkles in smaller ones sized to the headcount, and then keeps spending mid-shift so a second wave can show up an hour in. Some rounds it deliberately fails to buy anything and you get a quiet extended shift. Separately, ordinary crew get a blinking "cards" icon during the shift: click it and you pick one of a few offered personal side-quests, from silly ("go find out if the station really does make the best apple pie", "open three pieces of mail", "have a beer") to serious ("an assassin has been dispatched to kill you, survive thirty minutes"), with completion paying out credits or unlocking a hidden section in vending machines.

- **Take** — It solves the two biggest shape problems at once: rounds stop being one-antag-then-nothing, and non-antag crew get a steady drip of low-stakes personal goals that gives them something to do when their department is idle.
- **Skip** — The dynamic table is basically a manifest of Starlight's own antag roster (their changeling, vampire, devil, SELF agents, sub-wizard, cosmic cult) and the card system spans roughly 120 files with its own alert, selection window, task/handler/reward subsystems plus hooks into mail, faxes, ID cards, chat and terminator spawns, so you cannot take it without either porting or rewriting the antags it spends its budget on.
- **Touches** — all-players, antagonist, command, security, service, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [changeling](#changeling), [vampire](#vampire), [devil-pacts](#devil-pacts), [wizard-magic](#wizard-magic), [cosmic-cult](#cosmic-cult), [minor-antag-roles](#minor-antag-roles), [player-economy-and-banking](#player-economy-and-banking), [mail-delivery](#mail-delivery)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**ephemeral-space**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every shift the station itself is rolled fresh: lights and the space backdrop get a different tint (occasionally a dim "void" or a mellow nightshift palette), floors come pre-dirtied with graffiti, food wrappers and puddles of vomit, blood, welding fuel or worse, solar panels start cracked or missing, rats have gnawed holes in doors, and on a rare roll the whole camera is flipped 180 degrees for the round. On top of that, a "masquerade" is picked as the round's mode: it decides which secret-identity masks and hidden organizations are in play at the current population and which event schedulers run. During the round a vote panel appears for players holding the stagehand/voter role, with a ding and a chat announcement, letting them pick what the station gets next (next station event, next degradation event); the winning choice is announced to voters and immediately fires as a game rule.

- **Take** — It replaces "secret roll" with a director layer players can actually see and steer: rolled station variation makes every shift look and feel different from minute one, and the stagehand vote hands the pacing of events to players instead of a hidden scheduler.
- **Skip** — The masquerade half is the round mode for the fork's secret-identity/organization system and does almost nothing without it, and the vote framework is a whole bespoke UI plus vote-option type hierarchy you would then own; only the station-variation passes come out cleanly on their own.
- **Touches** — all-players, ghost-roles, antagonist, engineering, service, command
- **Needs** — [secret-identities](#secret-identities), [custom-station-events](#custom-station-events)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**sunrise**

*structural · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Instead of a fixed secret roll, the shift is run by a director that paces the round in phases: a quiet stretch, a build-up, a peak where the nastiest things land, then a recovery lull, looping for the whole shift. Players vote in the lobby between named intensity settings (Calm, Classic, Insane) that decide how much trouble the director can afford to buy and how long the calm stretches last, and the same director hands out helpful shifts too (pizza parties, gift crates of security guns, engineering and medical supply drops) alongside the hostile ones. How much the station gets thrown at it scales with how well-armed and well-stocked the crew actually is, so a fat, heavily-armed shift draws bigger threats than a skeleton one. At round end there is another vote for map and preset before the next shift starts.

- **Take** — It replaces the flat random-event drip with a paced narrative arc (calm, build, peak, recovery) plus a player vote on intensity, which is the single biggest lever on how a whole shift feels without writing any new content.
- **Skip** — The threat-cost table has to be hand-tuned per event for your own event roster, and it takes over round flow entirely, so any mistuning is felt by every player in every round rather than being contained to one system.
- **Touches** — all-players, antagonist, security, command, admin
- **Verified** — supported. storyteller_types.yml confirms the exact phase model (relaxation/buildUp/peak/recovery minutes) for Calm/Classic/Insane, each with distinct budgetModifier and armed-crew/security strength coefficients (strengthArmedCrewCoefficient, strengthSecurityCoefficient), matching the 'quiet/build/peak/recovery' and 'scales with how armed the crew is' claims. storyteller_metadata.yml confirms Helpful-type gifts (pizza parties large/small, security guns, engineering/medical/janitor gifts) at threatCost 0. game_presets.yml shows StorytellerCalm/Classic/Insane all have showInVote: true, confirming the lobby vote. RoundEndVoteSystem.cs confirms a round-end Map+Preset vote. Critically, for the graft_shape question: Content.Server/GameTicking/GameTicker.GamePreset.cs is an UPSTREAM file (no _Sunrise in its path) that contains a block explicitly marked '// Sunrise-Start ... // Sunrise-End' adding _excludedPresets/ExcludedPresets/AddExcludedPreset/ClearExcludedPresets members, which StorytellerSystem.Voting.cs consumes via GameTicker.ExcludedPresets. This is a real edit to an upstream-namespace file within the fork's own tree, contradicting the seed's zero-files-outside-namespace 'drop-in' premise and confirming 'fork-patches-upstream' rather than merely needing edits in a hypothetical target repo.

**stellar-station**

*structural · entangled · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every shift the station flies out to one of eight named hazard sectors (Apocryphal, Azure, Corporate, Crystalline, Magnetar, Malign, Pelagic, Supernova), each with its own starfield backdrop, coloured ambient lighting over the whole station, optional weather, and a soundtrack that changes in stages as the round wears on, with one sector even shifting the screen's colour grading in the final ten minutes. The sector also rolls a hidden "shift intensity" from green to black that decides how often random station events fire, and the gap between events keeps shrinking as the round goes on, so a black shift is a near-constant stream of trouble while a green one is quiet. Some sectors additionally roll a chance that a major threat is brewing, which quietly compresses the round to a shorter, randomised length instead of the usual ninety minutes. None of this is announced to the crew, so players read the shift from the sky, the light and the music rather than from text.

- **Take** — It makes every round feel like a different place with a different tempo using one lever (a sector pick plus an intensity scalar) rather than hand-tuning event lists, and the escalating event cadence gives rounds a natural build toward the end.
- **Skip** — The interesting half is not actually wired up yet: every sector's event table points at a placeholder (BasicGameRulesTable) and the per-sector antag sub-gamemode blocks are commented out, so today the eight sectors differ only in backdrop, light colour and music, and the whole thing sits on top of the fork's own game-rule base class, post-process LUT pipeline, roof-lighting and timer helpers plus a station that is a flyable shuttle grid FTL'd into a generated map.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, antagonist
- **Needs** — [station-and-environment-art](#station-and-environment-art), [visual-feedback-effects](#visual-feedback-effects), [custom-station-events](#custom-station-events)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**impstation**

*structural · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Instead of one "secret" roll, the shift can open in a named alternate mode: "Spy VS Spy", where every single crewmember wakes up as a traitor with codewords and a small uplink budget (0, 3 or 20 telecrystals depending on the variant) and objectives to kill another spy, keep a specific spy alive, and get off the station; "Pacifist VS Pacifist", the same all-against-all but with everyone unable to attack, so you have to kill through the environment while a telecrystal-eating fox named Renault wanders out of the vents as a bonus prize; and "All Aboard The Ship", which runs nukies, spies, revs, zombies and changelings simultaneously while every disaster scheduler including meteors and Kessler syndrome fires at once. Alongside those, hidden duplicate presets keep the "Secret" title and description so an admin can lock in a specific mode without the lobby knowing what is coming, presets can refuse to start if too many or too few players readied up, a lone pacified thief can be dropped in mid-round as a sub-gamemode, and a roundstart pass can quietly hand one random crewmember a hidden trait such as being able to perceive ghosts.

- **Take** — A handful of YAML preset definitions turn the existing antag rulesets you already ship into completely different round shapes (everyone-is-a-traitor, everyone-pacified, all-antags-at-once) plus admin-invisible secret variants, which is an enormous amount of round variety for very little new content.
- **Skip** — These are free-for-all modes that abandon normal station roleplay entirely and lean hard on population size, so they need per-server population tuning and admin discipline; the presets also reference antag rulesets (changeling, heretic) and objective prototypes a fork may not have, so half the list will not resolve without also grafting those.
- **Touches** — all-players, antagonist, admins, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [traitor-uplink](#traitor-uplink), [antag-objectives-and-uplink-rework](#antag-objectives-and-uplink-rework), [changeling](#changeling), [heretic](#heretic)
- **Verified** — supported. The gameplay text checks out in fine detail: game_presets.yml confirms SpyVsSpy/SpyVsSpy3TC/SpyVsSpy20TC give startingBalance 0/3/20 TC exactly as claimed, with KillRandomTraitorSvSObjective + RandomTraitorAliveSvSObjective + EscapeShuttleObjective matching 'kill a spy / keep one alive / escape'; PacifistVsPacifist carries EveryonePacifiedRule plus a RenaultMigrationEventScheduler spawning MobFoxRenault ('bonus TC' comment); AllAboardTheShip's rule list is literally Nukeops+SpyVsSpy20TC+Revolutionary+Zombie+Changeling+KesslerSyndromeScheduler+MeteorSwarm(Mild)Scheduler; the Secret* presets all carry name: secret-title/description: secret-description with a code comment 'this allows admins to set the gamemode without anyone knowing'; ThiefSingular's own comment says it 'will just apply Pacified to one person in the middle of the round'; and ModifyComponentsVariationPassComponent defaults MinMaxComponentRecievers to (1,1), matching 'one random crewmember'. On graft_shape: the seed's drop-in call does not survive. AllAboardTheShip and the Secret* presets resolve their antag rules against Resources/Prototypes/GameRules/roundstart.yml (a plain, non-underscored, upstream-named directory), and diffing impstation's Nukeops entity there against space-wizards/space-station-14's own GameRules/roundstart.yml shows the AntagSelection schema has diverged (prefRoles/fallbackRoles/spawnerPrototype/startingGear vs vanilla's antags:[FixedAntagCount/LinearAntagCount]+DynamicRuleCost) - the fork's own copy really does carry a modified file inside an upstream-named directory, not a self-contained addition. Separately, Changeling and HereticLowpop (needed by AllAboardTheShip and the two LowpopHeretics presets) live in Resources/Prototypes/_Goobstation/GameRules/roundstart.yml, a different fork namespace the cited evidence never mentions. Net: 'needs-upstream-edits' is the right call, but the skip_because's causal story overreaches - of the 8/16 presets referencing IDs outside _Impstation, only 3 (AllAboardTheShip, LowpopHeretics, SecretLowpopHeretics) are blocked by content a vanilla-based fork genuinely lacks (Goobstation's rework); the other 5 Secret* presets reference Nukeops/Revolutionary/Zombie/Wizard, standard SS14 content every fork already ships, which would resolve fine unless the receiving fork's AntagSelection schema has also diverged the way impstation's has.
  - unsupported: the presets also reference antag rulesets (changeling, heretic) and objective prototypes a fork may not have, so half the list will not resolve without also grafting those

### Wizard and spellcasting

<a id="wizard-magic"></a>

Magic-using antagonist with a purchasable spellbook, staves, polymorph forms, summoned familiars and apprentices.

**goobstation**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 5 forks carry it*

A wizard arrives on the station with a book of spells and a pile of magic gear, and spends the round doing openly absurd, high-power things to the crew: turning people into animals with a staff of change, throwing lightning packets, teleporting across the station with a scroll or a target-picker window, turning into an unstoppable roaring hulk, spying on rooms through a scrying orb, and fighting with enchanted swords, a supermatter halberd or a bolt-action rifle that fires magic bullets. Victims polymorphed into animals actually start speaking in that animal's noises, and there is a joke "chuuni" mode where every spell you cast is announced with a florid anime incantation. The wizard can also sign apprenticeship contracts that pull ghosts in as junior wizards, each with a themed spell loadout (destruction, ethereal, summoning, gunslinger, arcane, sanguine, robeless, or completely random), who are told to protect their master at all costs.

- **Take** — It turns the wizard from a single antag into a whole round archetype, with a real spell economy, a refundable purchase store, and team-antag apprentice ghost roles that give spectators something to do mid-round.
- **Skip** — It is roughly 666 files across three assemblies including a fork-only Content.Goobstation.Common magic layer and client-side UI for mirror, refund, spellblade and teleport, plus stray cross-links into Goob's Lavaland content, so it cannot be lifted as a folder and will need continuous re-merging.
- **Touches** — antagonist, ghost-roles, security, all-players, command
- **Needs** — [lavaland-mining-planet](#lavaland-mining-planet)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**frontier**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 5 forks carry it*

Robed Wizard Federation casters exist as a hostile faction to fight rather than a wizard antag round: apprentices lob bars of soap and summoned elementals, adepts sling ice bolts, fireballs and thrown bricks, and masters chain lightning or burn you down with a caustic beam, muttering to themselves between shots. Kill one and its robes and staff drop as loot, and a staff charger machine lets you refill a captured staff and throw the same magic back at whatever comes next. The spells are shouted-incantation summons that wink out after three minutes: a magic vending machine, a blue-curacao elemental that imprints on whoever conjured it and fights alongside them, or a shade and a drained one borrowed from the fork's cult content.

- **Take** — It reframes wizardry as lootable PvE content — a three-tier enemy faction with distinct elemental attack patterns, plus rechargeable staves the crew can capture and actually cast with — instead of a single-player antag round.
- **Skip** — It leans on Frontier's own hostile-mob and faction scaffolding (a custom hostilewizard inventory template, WizFedFaction, a heretic damage modifier set) and two of the four conjure spells summon blood-cult mobs, so grafting means porting or stubbing those first; and the wizards are dead weight until you also have expeditions or ruins to place them in.
- **Touches** — salvage, security, expedition-crews, all-players
- **Needs** — [blood-cult](#blood-cult), [heretic](#heretic)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Changeling

<a id="changeling"></a>

Shapeshifting antagonist that absorbs crew DNA to copy identities, spends evolution points on biological weapons and revives from apparent death.

**goobstation**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 5 forks carry it*

A changeling looks and sounds exactly like a crewmate until it doesn't. It grabs someone hard, holds them down and absorbs them, leaving a hollow husk that reads "the body feels hollow" on examine, and it can then wear that person's face and name for the rest of the round. It runs on two bars instead of an uplink: chemicals that slowly refill and pay for abilities, and biomass that drains until it eats, warning "we begin to hunger" and eventually killing it. Points spent in an evolution menu buy an arm blade, claw or hammer that erupts from the body, chitinous armour and an organic shield, a dart gun, deafening shrieks that break lights and radios, and silent stings that blind, mute, slow or chill a victim from a distance. Escape hatches include instant wound-and-broken-bone regeneration, a stun-proof adrenaline surge, adaptation to darkness and to freezing vacuum, going translucent, and dropping into regenerative stasis so it plays dead and gets back up unless someone defibs or absorbs the corpse. As a last resort it abandons the body entirely and skitters away as a vent-crawling head slug. Lings share a private Hivemind radio channel, and their objectives are absorb N humanoids, absorb rival changelings, collect unique genomes, or escape on the shuttle wearing a specific person's identity.

- **Take** — It is a complete, mature antagonist with its own economy, shop, identity theft, fake-death loop and objectives, and it reshapes rounds by making the crew distrust faces rather than hunt for contraband.
- **Skip** — Absorbing requires a hard grab, so the whole kill loop assumes Goob's grab/combat escalation rather than upstream pulling, and the ~230 files of abilities, stings, armour, shields, dart gun and head slug mob are a large permanent maintenance surface that also collides with upstream's own in-progress changeling.
- **Touches** — antagonist, security, medical, all-players
- **Needs** — [close-quarters-combat-rework](#close-quarters-combat-rework), [reagent-and-reaction-expansion](#reagent-and-reaction-expansion)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Alternate round types

<a id="alternate-round-types"></a>

Whole round scenarios that replace the standard station shift instead of modifying it: their own map or grids, their own player roles and spawn flow, and their own win, escape or elimination condition. Covers crash-survival and prison-planet rounds, assault-operative rounds, and team-versus-team PvP modes with team cores, capture points and dedicated arenas. A framework that only picks or weights which rules run inside a normal shift is `round-modifier-presets`; a single antagonist role rolled into a normal shift keeps its own antag id.

### Xenomorph hive

<a id="xenomorph-hive"></a>

Alien hive faction with facehugger infection, chestbursting, larva growth, caste evolution, plasma, acid and resin structures.

**rmc14, white-dream**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 5 forks carry it*

A facehugger leaps onto someone's face and latches on too tight to pull off; over the next several minutes the victim feels their chest hurt, then their throat go sore, then something crawling inside them, until a larva bursts out of their chest in front of everyone and a dead crewmember becomes a new alien. Aliens talk to each other over a private Hivemind channel, build up until they are "ready to evolve", and grow into stronger castes (drone, hunter, sentinel, praetorian, queen) with only one queen allowed at a time, while the queen can hand-pick an adult child for promotion. The bigger RMC-14 version goes much further: aliens spend plasma to grow resin weeds and hive structures (a hive core, egg morphers that fling parasites at anyone who walks close, healing nodes, plasma trees, pylons), dig named tunnels to fast-travel across the map, hide acid and parasite traps under floor debris, and heavy castes like the boiler lob acid or neurotoxin gas globs from off-screen. The stated goal is that only one species walks off the station.

- **Take** — It converts dead and captured crew into more antagonists, so the round has a real escalating losing condition instead of a fixed antag count, and every death makes the threat bigger.
- **Skip** — It is two very different-sized things wearing one name: White Dream's is a ~180-file antag you could plausibly maintain, while RMC-14's ~3400-file version is written for its marine-vs-xeno total conversion and its guides openly lean on orbital bombardment, close air support, barricade lines and host-capture nests that a normal station round simply does not have.
- **Touches** — antagonist, ghost-roles, all-players, medical, security
- **Needs** — [alternate-round-types](#alternate-round-types)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.
