### Limb wounds, body-part targeting and surgery

<a id="surgery-and-limb-wounds"></a>

Replaces flat mob damage with per-limb health and wounds, a body-part targeting doll, step-driven surgical procedures with tool and condition requirements, and detachable limbs and organs.

**rmc14**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 8 forks carry it*

Getting hurt leaves visible wounds rather than just a damage number: your injuries show in brackets until someone treats them, and an untreated body keeps bleeding and never heals on its own. A medic bandages, salves, sutures or grafts you with trauma kits, burn kits, gauze, ointment, surgical line or synth-graft, at which point you start slowly regenerating and drugs work faster; a health scanner reads out your brute/burn/tox/oxy load and prints plain-language advice like "use trauma kits on the lacerated areas" or "connect a blood pack". Deeper problems need real surgery: lay the patient on an operating table, use a surgical tool on them, pick which body part to work on, and walk the ordered steps (scalpel incision, retractor, drill, hemostat, cautery to close) for procedures such as opening the ribcage or cutting an alien embryo out of a chest, with the harder operations gated to actually-trained doctors instead of nurses or corpsmen.

- **Take** — It turns healing from "click the patient with the right chem" into a multi-step, tool-and-table procedure that gives medical staff a real minute-to-minute job and makes injuries persist until someone actually works on them.
- **Skip** — RMC-14's version is built for its own game: it replaces the whole healing item set with CM-prefixed duplicates, gates surgeries on RMC's job-skill tiers, and its headline procedures are xenomorph embryo and alien heart removal, so porting it means either importing those systems or rewriting the content layer around a generic surgery framework.
- **Touches** — medical, all-players, antagonist
- **Needs** — [squads-ranks-and-skills](#squads-ranks-and-skills), [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**starlight**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 8 forks carry it*

A doctor holds a patient still and works through a menu of named operations, each a checklist of steps that must be done in order with the right tool: cut with the scalpel, clamp bleeders with the hemostat, hold the skin open with the retractor, then locate, clamp and lift out the liver or appendix before closing everything back up. Limbs and heads are real detachable objects, so a surgeon can saw an arm off, carry it away, and later sew a replacement arm, leg or head onto an empty socket by exposing nerves and vessels and rejoining them. Slime people and IPCs get their own versions of the same operations (coagulate the jelly, unscrew the plating and disconnect coolant channels), and the same surgical UI is how eye, hand and brain implants get installed, giving the patient permanent perks like welding-flash immunity or built-in medical HUD vision. Some melee weapons can also lop a limb clean off mid-fight.

- **Take** — It turns medical from "click patient with medipen" into a multi-tool, multi-step procedure with real stakes, and it doubles as the only sane delivery mechanism for cybernetic limbs and implants.
- **Skip** — It replaces the whole physiology stack (its own bloodstream, respirator, metabolizer, thermal regulator and body systems) and rewires every species' body and organ prototypes, so grafting it means owning mob health forever and re-doing the work on every upstream sync.
- **Touches** — medical, science, antagonist, all-players
- **Needs** — [cybernetic-augments](#cybernetic-augments)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 8 forks carry it*

A surgeon lays surgery drapes on a strapped-down patient, which opens a puppet-style body chart where you pick the head or torso and then walk a branching operation tree: cut the skin with a scalpel, clamp the bleeders with a hemostat, retract, saw through skull or ribs, apply bone gel, and cauterize the wound shut, each step with its own tool requirement, timer and wet surgical sound, and each step actively bleeding the patient until you clamp it. Once you have the chest or skull open you can do things you cannot do with chemicals: pull shrapnel out of a gunshot wound with a magnetic extractor, heal brain damage directly, shock a stopped heart with a compact hand defibrillator pressed straight onto it, scan for hidden implants and print a report, and (if you are a syndicate agent who knows how) cut out a mindslave implant's effects or change a patient's voice. Non-surgeons get blocked by the requirement checks: no scalpel, no scanner, patient still wearing armour, or nothing actually wrong with the organ, and the game tells you which one failed.

- **Take** — It turns the doctor role from "apply the correct chemical" into a genuine multi-step procedure with tools, timing and failure states, and it is data-driven enough that new operations are YAML graph edges rather than new C# systems.
- **Skip** — The operation graphs are wired straight into other SS220 systems (a pathology/wound-condition model, clinical-death revival timers, TTS voice swapping, their mindslave antag implant), so the interesting steps only work if you bring those along or stub them out, and the client side is a bespoke node-graph UI you would then own forever.
- **Touches** — medical, science, antagonist, security, all-players
- **Needs** — [medical-status-conditions](#medical-status-conditions), [resuscitation-and-life-support](#resuscitation-and-life-support), [text-to-speech](#text-to-speech)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Long-running medical conditions

<a id="medical-status-conditions"></a>

Persistent character conditions such as allergies, addictions, chronic pain, blood types and numbness that create ongoing treatment work rather than one-off damage.

**backmen**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Every character carries a mood meter shown as a face icon in the alerts bar, running from Perfect down through Neutral to Horrible and Insane; clicking it opens a list of the things currently helping or hurting your mood. Being hungry, thirsty, hurt, on fire, suffocating, handcuffed, slipped, vomiting, pied, or missing a limb after surgery drags it down, while hugs, a good sleep, an arcade session, a chaplain's blessing, petting an animal, saving someone (or being saved) push it back up, and letting it sink far enough makes you walk slower. Drugs like nicotine, space drugs and happiness give a big short-term mood high followed by a long withdrawal slump, so repeat users end up chasing the next dose. Separately, actually starving builds escalating chest pain that keeps growing while you go unfed and eventually starts damaging organs, so a hungry crewmate becomes a medical problem rather than just a slow one.

- **Take** — It turns hunger, injury, restraint and drug use into a single persistent state players can see and other players can actively improve, giving service, chaplain and medical roles real reasons to interact with healthy crew.
- **Skip** — The hunger-pain half is wired straight into Backmen's own pain, consciousness and trauma systems, so it cannot come without that whole surgery rework, and even the mood half needs hooks scattered through upstream hugging, slipping, vomiting, arcade, blessing, hunger/thirst and movement-speed code.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, service, chaplain, security, antagonist
- **Needs** — [surgery-and-limb-wounds](#surgery-and-limb-wounds), [character-traits](#character-traits)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Taking recreational drugs leaves traces in your blood for about ten minutes after the last dose, and anyone holding a narcotics test swab can grab a suspect, spend a few seconds swabbing them, and then read the result off the used swab: positive or negative, with the subject's DNA left on it as evidence. Separately, the body remembers every chemical it has processed, keeping a per-drug tolerance counter that builds with repeated doses and fades on its own after a while, so a character who keeps hitting the same drug is no longer the same patient as one taking it for the first time. The tolerance counter is bookkeeping only in the files here; the actual "the drug does less for you now" part lives in whichever chemical effects choose to read it.

- **Take** — It turns drug use into something with consequences a second player can act on: security gets a real, DNA-tagged drug test instead of guessing, and repeat users build a tolerance the round tracks.
- **Skip** — The interesting half is missing from these files: tolerance is a counter nobody in this set consumes, and the "you used a narcotic" signal has to be raised from chemistry code that isn't fork-namespaced, so you inherit an upstream patch plus SS220's CLA-licensed source with a hosting restriction that will not sit comfortably next to AGPL fork code.
- **Touches** — medical, security, all-players
- **Verified** — supported. Cloned the repo and grepped for the actual wiring: Content.Shared/Metabolism/MetabolizerSystem.cs (upstream namespace/path, no SS220 in it) is patched in place with "// ss220 add narcotics test start/end" comments that raise MetabolizeNarcoticEvent when a reagent's Group == NarcoticsGroup — this is the only place the event is raised. Likewise Content.Shared/EntityEffects/Effects/StatusEffects/MovementSpeedModifierEntityEffectSystem.cs (also upstream path) is patched with "SS220 add movement speed modification depending on the adaptation" to call ChemicalAdaptationSystem.TryGetMetabolized. Both are genuine upstream-directory files carrying fork edits, which directly falsifies the seed's drop-in/zero-files-outside-namespace path-data guess and confirms the claim's own "needs-upstream-edits" call, right down to the specific mechanism it named (narcotic signal raised from non-fork-namespaced chemistry code).

### Cybernetic augments and prosthetics

<a id="cybernetic-augments"></a>

Installable robotic limbs, organs and combat augments with implanter kits, autosurgeons and a character-creation picker for roundstart cybernetics.

**goobstation**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 6 forks carry it*

A surgeon can cut open your arm or chest and install machinery inside you: a battery slot with its own charge alert on your HUD, a recharger that tops it up from an APC or by standing on a charging pad, and augments that live in your limbs. The headline one is a tool panel arm, where a utility, botany, surgery or paperwork toolkit is loaded before installation and then swapped through a radial menu, with each tool sliding out of your forearm in place of your hand ("the crowbar emerges from your arm") and retracting when you switch, so you can never be disarmed of it but also can't hand it to anyone. Other implants are pure combat toys: mantis blades that pop out of an arm, a berserker implant you trigger to shrug off damage and hit harder for a few seconds while trailing red afterimages and jittering, a Sandevistan speed implant with its own screen glitch overlay, and a recoil absorber arm that steadies your guns. A single-use autosurgeon can be applied to install or replace a part on yourself without a doctor. Everything runs off your internal battery, so a flat cell means dead augments.

- **Take** — Tool-panel arms give engineers, botanists, surgeons and paper-pushers a permanent, undroppable version of their kit that has to be surgically earned and kept charged, which is a genuinely new reason to visit medical mid-round.
- **Skip** — It is built on top of the Shitmed surgery and body-part rework (surgery step prototypes, organ add/remove events, BodyEffects), so it cannot be lifted without that whole system underneath it, and the combat implants (berserker, Sandevistan) drag in their own client overlays and balance questions.
- **Touches** — medical, science, engineering, botany, service, security, antagonist, all-players
- **Needs** — [surgery-and-limb-wounds](#surgery-and-limb-wounds)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**starlight**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 6 forks carry it*

At character creation you can spend a points budget on robotic limbs and organs, and you spawn with them already fitted: mechanical arms, hands, legs and feet that visibly replace your flesh ones with a choice of chassis looks (plain, silver, steel, security red), some of them specialised, a surgeon's hand with built-in tools, an engineer's tool hand, sprinter legs. Cyber organs come with drawbacks as well as perks, so a synthetic gut can leave you unable to taste anything you eat or make you get thirsty far faster. The counterplay is disruption: a handheld disruptor pressed against you for a second, or a shot from a disruption round, gives you a "Cybernetic Disruption" status icon that switches off every active ability your cyberware grants until it wears off, so an officer can strip an augmented brawler back down to a normal human mid-fight.

- **Take** — It gives augmentation real two-sided play, roundstart cyberware you pick and show off, plus a disruptor counter that lets security and antags temporarily unplug it, instead of augments being a pure one-way power gain.
- **Skip** — It is built on top of Starlight's own limb/body-part rework and its character-creation UI, so the visible sprite swaps, the roundstart point-buy picker and the organ effects all assume systems a fork would have to bring across first; the cyberlimb art is also a large per-species sprite set (separate Resomi and Rodentia sheets) that has to be maintained for every species you add.
- **Touches** — medical, security, engineering, all-players, antagonist
- **Needs** — [surgery-and-limb-wounds](#surgery-and-limb-wounds), [lobby-and-character-ui](#lobby-and-character-ui)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**deltav**

*activity · entangled · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

In character setup you can give your character robotic arms, hands, legs and feet from two different "manufacturers", each with recolourable panel layers, so a crewmate can walk around visibly part-machine without any mechanical change. In the round itself this is a grab-bag of new things you can have injected under your skin with an implanter gun: an implant that lets you speak in any voice you can imagine (with a harpy-only singing variant), a watchdog implant that automatically screams your name and location over the security channel the moment you crit or die, a hostage implant that pacifies you but makes syndicate turrets hold their fire, a CentComm mindshield that also makes you an invalid target, hidden uplinks for nuclear operatives and NanoTrasen agents, an implant that lets a hitman conjure calling cards on demand, a true-death implant that makes your corpse unrevivable, and a bluespace lifeline that yanks your body somewhere safe the instant you die. Delta-V does not add surgical prosthetic limbs with their own health here; the "cybernetics" are cosmetic limb skins plus an expanded implant catalogue.

- **Take** — The implant half is cheap, self-contained gear that instantly widens what antags and security can carry, and the cyberlimb markings let players look augmented with no balance cost at all.
- **Skip** — It is a shopping list rather than a system: several implants (hitman calling cards, NanoTrasen agent uplink, conspiracy radio channel, hostage/turret behaviour) are inert props unless you also run the fork antag roles they were built for, and the "cybernetic limbs" are purely cosmetic skins, so anyone expecting real prosthetics will be disappointed.
- **Touches** — antagonist, security, medical, all-players
- **Needs** — [minor-antag-roles](#minor-antag-roles)
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as drop-in, corrected by the verifier. Every cited file exists and the implant/marking catalogue reads as described, but the unit's own YAML hard-references other fork systems that would fail prototype load if grafted alone: parent StorePresetNanolink and NanoCoin currency (NT-agent store), the Harmony Conspiracy radio channel, the Hostage faction from _DV/ai_factions.yml, and a HarpySinger component whitelist (harpy species, an undeclared dependency), so drop-in overstates liftability; no fork patches to upstream files were found (syndicate turrets carry no Hostage wiring and AntagImmune exists upstream), so entangled, not needs-upstream-edits, is the right downgrade.
  - unsupported: "with a harpy-only singing variant" — BionicSyrinxImplant is harpy-only (whitelist: HarpySinger) but the file only says it lets a harpy adjust their voice; nothing in the evidence ties it to singing.
  - unsupported: "automatically screams your name and location over the security channel" — the cited yml shows only RattleOnTrigger to the Security channel on Critical/Dead; the name-and-location message content is not in any cited file (it lives in upstream rattle locale/behavior).

## Chemistry

### Reagent and reaction expansion

<a id="reagent-and-reaction-expansion"></a>

Adds reagents, reactions, flavour profiles and glassware on top of the base chemistry set, covering medicines, toxins, narcotics and novelty chemicals.

**rmc14**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 13 forks carry it*

Replaces the standard medicine shelf with a Colonial-Marines-style drug set: bicaridine for brute, kelotane and dermaline for burns, dexalin and dexalin plus for suffocation, dylovene for toxins, tricordrazine as the weak all-rounder, plus specialty picks like inaprovaline, epinephrine, leporazine and cryoxadone. Every one of them has an overdose and a critical-overdose line, so a medic who doubles up on a screaming patient starts doing brute damage instead of healing, and dosing becomes a judgement call rather than "empty the syringe". Alongside the medicines it adds recreational narcotics that make you laugh, see rainbows, jitter and eventually black out, and a bench of incendiary ordnance chemistry (sticky napalm, high-combustion napalm, B-gel, CLF3, sulphuric acid) that a technician mixes into flamethrower and incinerator fuel of varying nastiness. An in-game guidebook lists every reagent by group so players can look up exactly what a chemical does before drinking it.

- **Take** — The overdose / critical-overdose tiering turns healing into a dosing decision with a real downside, which gives medics an actual skill ceiling instead of a syringe-emptying reflex, and it comes with player-readable guidebook entries generated from the effects themselves.
- **Skip** — It is a replacement set, not an addition — the CM-named drugs cover the same ground as upstream's medicines, so you either displace the existing chemistry (rewiring every medkit, vendor, dispenser and chem recipe that references the old reagents) or ship two parallel medicine sets that confuse everyone; the incendiary half is also only meaningful if your fork already has flamethrowers and spreading fire to burn things with.
- **Touches** — medical, science, cargo, security, all-players
- **Needs** — [spreading-fire-system](#spreading-fire-system)
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as needs upstream edits, corrected by the verifier. All cited evidence files do live entirely under _RMC14 namespace directories (Resources/Prototypes/_RMC14/Reagents/, Resources/ServerInfo/Guidebook/_RMC14/, Content.Shared/_RMC14/Chemistry/), and the new reagents merely inherit vanilla reagent IDs (Bicaridine, Dexalin, etc.) via YAML `parent:` — so it does not literally patch upstream files, contradicting a plain 'fork-patches-upstream' reading. But reading the actual effect classes shows the reagent system is built on RMC14's own parallel damage/health/status/zombie infrastructure (SharedRMCDamageableSystem, SharedRMCBloodstreamSystem, a bespoke 'Genetic' damage group, RMCUnconsciousComponent, CureZombieInfection) rather than vanilla SS14 mechanics, so a graft needs to bring that whole substrate along, not just point vendors/medkits at new reagent IDs as the claim's 'skip_because' implies. That is a deeper coupling than 'needs-upstream-edits' captures.
  - unsupported: "Every one of them has an overdose and a critical-overdose line" — false per the cited medicine.yml itself: CMCryoxadone, CMClonexadone, RMCMethylphenidate, RMCCitalopram, and RMCAntiZed have no overdose/criticalOverdose fields at all.
  - unsupported: "a medic who doubles up on a screaming patient starts doing brute damage instead of healing" — verified against RMCChemicalEffect.cs (base TickOverdose/TickCriticalOverdose are no-op virtuals) plus Antitoxic.cs and Neogenetic.cs: overdose consequences vary per effect type. Bicaridine's Neogenetic effect deals BURN damage on overdose (not brute); Dylovene's Antitoxic overdose tier is a literal commented-out TODO no-op; only the Biocidic effect (used by Arithrazine/RussianRed specifically) deals brute damage. There is no universal 'brute damage instead of healing' consequence.
  - unsupported: depends_on lists only 'spreading-fire-system', but the reagent effect implementations (Antitoxic.cs, Neogenetic.cs, RMCAntiZed's CureZombieInfection) show real dependencies on RMC14-only shared systems: SharedRMCDamageableSystem.DistributeHealingCached, SharedRMCBloodstreamSystem, a custom 'Genetic' damage group, RMCUnconsciousComponent, and RMC14's own zombie-infection cure mechanic — none of which are vanilla SS14 or guaranteed to exist in a target fork.

**impstation**

*activity · entangled · medium confidence · 13 forks carry it*

Bartenders and chemists get a much longer menu: sports-drink "Asterade" in eight colours with its own bottle sprites, novelty cocktails and coffees that each pour into their own custom glass, and joke drinks with teeth (one of them just bludgeons you for a hundred blunt damage once you've had twenty units of it). Drugs are written for the bit rather than for the stat block, so a hit of "foreverweed" spams you with paranoid hallucinations about the Captain, CentComm and the space cops knowing you're high, another fills your gut with butterflies and anxious intrusive thoughts, and another tells you there are ants biting you from the inside. On the medical side there is synthflesh that patches brute damage at the cost of cellular damage and random vomiting, dinosaur blood that works as a weak all-round heal, and hormone reagents (testosterone, estradiol). Every alien species on the station bleeds a distinct, named fluid you can bottle and run through a centrifuge to crack it into salt, copper, sugar, fat and, in one case, an energy drink, and creatures with thick shells simply cannot be injected at all ("Exoskeleton too thick!").

- **Take** — It is a large, mostly additive content drop that immediately gives bartenders, chemists and medics more to make and drink, and the drug flavour text does real roleplay work rather than just moving numbers.
- **Skip** — Roughly half of it is species blood and metabolizer plumbing for Impstation's own alien roster (Kodepiia, Allulalo, Thaven, Apid, Anomalocarid, Gray, snails), so without those species you inherit reagents nothing on your station actually bleeds, plus a long tail of new drink RSIs to carry.
- **Touches** — service, medical, science, all-players
- **Needs** — [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species), [non-humanoid-species-pack](#non-humanoid-species-pack)
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as needs upstream edits, corrected by the verifier. Every one of the 15 cited evidence files, plus the species entity files I additionally checked (e.g. Resources/Prototypes/_Impstation/Entities/Mobs/Species/allulalo.yml, which sets Bloodstream.bloodReferenceSolution to BloodAllulalo), lives entirely under _Impstation/ namespace directories — no upstream file is touched or needs to be, so 'needs-upstream-edits' is not supported. What is real is a functional dependency on two sibling fork-only units (the alien species packs) for roughly half the reagent content (the species bloods and their centrifuge breakdowns) to do anything in play; the rest (Asterade, novelty drinks, drugs, synthflesh, dino blood, hormones) works standalone. That is an inter-unit (fork-to-fork) entanglement, not a requirement to edit vanilla/upstream files.
  - unsupported: "sports-drink 'Asterade' in eight colours" — asterade.yml defines 9 colored variants (Orange, Purple, Red, Green, Yellow, Teal, Blue, Pink, Black), not eight.
  - unsupported: "Every alien species on the station bleeds a distinct, named fluid you can bottle and run through a centrifuge to crack it into salt, copper, sugar, fat and, in one case, an energy drink" — only 2 of the 5 centrifuge breakdown reactions in biological.yml actually produce that salt/copper/sugar/fat pattern (KodepiiaBloodBreakdown: Ice/Copper/TableSalt/Ammonia/Fat; AnomalocaridCopperBloodBreakdown: TableSalt/Copper/Sugar/Fat/EnergyDrink). GrayBloodBreakdown yields only Radium, SynthBloodBreakdown yields Silicon/Lithium/Ethanol/SpaceLube, and ReaperBloodBreakdown yields Ash/Fresium — none of those three match the claimed salt/copper/sugar/fat template at all.
  - unsupported: "novelty cocktails and coffees that each pour into their own custom glass" — of the 6 drinks in drinks.yml, AppleMartiniCrystalLite and CafeLatteVanilla reuse pre-existing vanilla sprite paths (Objects/Consumable/Drinks/martiniglassnoolive.rsi and cafe_latte.rsi, no _Impstation prefix), not custom glasses made for this expansion.

**serbia-strong**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 13 forks carry it*

Bartenders and chemists get a shelf of new drinks and chemicals that actually do things: Bacchus' Blessing hammers you drunk and shaking in a couple of sips, Vodka Kazaki slowly clears radiation, Hot Arachne scalds you from the inside, Bankate burns bare skin when you spill it, and each one pours into its own custom glass with its own tasting note ("like death and rocks", "like sweet dreams"). On the medical and combat side there are chems that make you hallucinate and a counter-chem that clears the hallucinations, chems that temporarily raise or lower how much damage you can soak before you crit, stamina and brain-damage chems, and a tolerance mechanic where repeated doses of the same reagent weaken or change its effect. Delivery gear comes with it: an advanced mini syringe dart that keeps pumping its payload into whoever it sticks in, single-use autoinjectors, pre-filled lead and uranium vials, and clothing that stops syringe darts from landing. A separate block of reagents belongs to SS220's mushroom-cult antag (conversion blood, a liberation elixir that unmutes converts, and a screaming acid brewed by cult monsters).

- **Take** — It is a big pile of independently-choosable content: you can lift three cocktails and a hallucinogen without lifting anything else, and each reagent is a self-contained YAML block.
- **Skip** — Every SS220 file carries a "© SS220, EULA/CLA with a hosting restriction" header rather than an open licence, so copying any of it into an AGPL fork is a licensing decision before it is an engineering one; on top of that the cult reagents are dead weight without SS220's mushroom-cult antag, and some strings are still untranslated Russian.
- **Touches** — service, medical, science, security, antagonist, all-players
- **Needs** — [blood-cult](#blood-cult)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**sunrise**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 13 forks carry it*

Chemists get a handful of new drugs to mix and a new way to hand them out: a hemostatic powder that clamps bleeding fast but poisons you past ten units, an anti-radiation drug that leaves the patient seeing rainbows, and a pressure-proofing stimulant that makes you jitter, stutter, scream and occasionally vomit while it keeps you alive in vacuum. The chem master can now print labelled medicated patches alongside pills and bottles, so medics can slap a dose on someone instead of injecting. Bartenders also get a run of real cocktails (Negroni, Old Fashioned, Mai Tai, Paloma, Dark and Stormy, Boyarsky, Yorsh and others) with their own flavour text, plus extra sodas and condiments.

- **Take** — The patch printer is a genuinely new delivery format for chemistry, and the cocktail list is free bartender depth that costs nothing but YAML.
- **Skip** — The medical reagents are written against Sunrise's custom Mangleness damage type and their status-effect prototypes, so they will not compile or balance sensibly without dragging in the fork's wound model; several of the listed recipe files are empty placeholders, so the headline file count overstates what is actually there.
- **Touches** — medical, science, service, all-players
- **Needs** — [surgery-and-limb-wounds](#surgery-and-limb-wounds)
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched all 10 cited files plus the base (non-_Sunrise) damage-type files. Every headline specific in the claim checks out verbatim in Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Chemistry/medicine.yml: HemostaticPowder does ModifyBleed -4.2 and adds Poison damage only once a ReagentCondition min:10.1 is met ("poisons past ten units"); Retroindralyne heals Radiation -3 and triggers StatusEffectSeeingRainbow ("seeing rainbows"); BarozinePlus grants a PressureImmunity GenericStatusEffect plus Jitter, a Stutter component, a Scream Emote, and probability-gated Vomit ("jitter, stutter, scream and occasionally vomit... keeps you alive in vacuum"). Content.Server/_Sunrise/Chemistry/EntitySystems/ChemMasterSystem.Patches.cs literally implements OnCreatePatchesMessage, spawning and labelling "Patch" entities from the chem master — confirms patch printing. Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Reagents/Consumable/Drink/alcohol.yml contains Boyarsky, DarkAndStormy, MaiTai, Negroni, OldFashioned, Paloma, Yorsh — every cocktail named in the claim is present. The three "empty placeholder" files (Recipes/narcotics.yml, Recipes/fun.yml, Reagents/fun.yml) really do return HTTP 200 with zero bytes, exactly matching the skip_because note about inflated file counts. On graft_shape: the seed's drop-in guess is wrong and the assessor's needs-upstream-edits downgrade is not just correct but understates how entangled it is. I traced the Mangleness damage type used by HemostaticPowder/Retroindralyne to Resources/Prototypes/_Sunrise/Damage/types.yml, and found the fork's own (non-_Sunrise) Resources/Prototypes/Damage/containers.yml has a "Mangleness # Sunrise-Edit" line spliced into an existing damageContainer — an in-place edit of an upstream-owned file, invisible to path-based scoping. Separately, the patch-printing feature required editing three more upstream files in place: Content.Server/Chemistry/EntitySystems/ChemMasterSystem.cs (marked partial, new SubscribeLocalEvent for ChemMasterCreatePatchesMessage, extra UI-state argument), and Content.Shared/Chemistry/SharedChemMaster.cs (new ChemMasterCreatePatchesMessage class, new PatchDosageLimit field on the existing UI state), all tagged "Sunrise-Edit start/end". This is direct proof of fork-patches-upstream (the fork's own tree already touches non-namespaced upstream files), and by the same token any integration into a different codebase would need equivalent edits to that codebase's damage-container and ChemMaster files, so integration-would-require-upstream-edits also holds.
  - unsupported: "so medics can slap a dose on someone instead of injecting" is a plausible but unverified inference — I confirmed the patch-printing UI/server flow (OnCreatePatchesMessage, labelling, dosage) but did not fetch the Patch entity prototype or its interaction code to confirm topical/touch application vs. some other consumption mechanic.

### Extra playable species

<a id="extra-playable-species"></a>

Additional playable species that reuse the upstream humanoid rig: the standard MobHumanoid-derived body prototype, the standard humanoid inventory template and the standard humanoid sprite layers, with the fork supplying only new species prototypes, organs, name datasets, markings, sprites and guidebook entries. Alien flavour does not move a species out of this id, body plan does: if it still wears a jumpsuit on a two-arm/two-leg doll, it is here. A species that ships its own body-part graph, its own inventory template or a non-bipedal rig belongs to `non-humanoid-species-pack`, and a multi-species pack is filed whole, never split across the two ids.

**corvax, cosmatic-drift, gaby-station-now-dumont-station, impstation, monolith, rmc14, serbia-strong**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 13 forks carry it*

At character creation you can pick from extra species beyond the upstream roster (cat-people, foxfolk, rat-people, bird-like Avali, shrimp-like Anomalocarids, hulking Ungu, shapeshifting Kodepiia, Thaven, Protogen and more), each with its own sprites, name pools, markings and a guidebook page explaining how it plays. Choosing one changes how your shift actually feels: Avali survive down to -50C but get shoved over easily, Rodentia stash small items in their cheeks and talk gibberish while doing it and starve a third faster, Ungu are too bulky to wear any hardsuit so must use two-piece EVA suits and stay conscious to 150 damage, Kodepiia can only digest raw meat and choke on waste gas, and Anomalocarids must periodically vent steam or overheat. Chemists, doctors and quartermasters have to account for species that burn in the heat, freeze in the cold, or refuse the standard food and suits.

- **Take** — Species are the single most visible identity lever a fork has, and the packs are mostly data (species prototypes, organs, name datasets, markings, sprites, guidebook pages) with real mechanical texture already tuned and documented for players.
- **Skip** — Every new humanoid silhouette drags a long tail of upstream work: per-species clothing/hardsuit sprite layers, loadout and job wiring, markings points, food and atmos balance, plus rules text (the Impstation entries lean on server-specific naming and RP rules), and each species is permanent maintenance against upstream humanoid churn.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, cargo, service, engineering, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [character-markings-and-editor](#character-markings-and-editor), [assigned-roleplay-moods](#assigned-roleplay-moods)
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched all 10 cited files successfully and every specific mechanical number checks out exactly: Avali -50C to 36C plus 5% shove vulnerability, Rodentia cheek-storage/unintelligible speech plus 33%-faster hunger ('a third faster'), Ungu barred from NT hardsuits (must use EVA/Emergency/Ancient-EVA two-piece suits) and critical at exactly 150 damage, Kodepiia digesting only raw/uncooked meat and choking on inhaled waste gasses, Anomalocarid needing to vent excess heat as water vapor or spiral into overheating. All 10 evidence paths sit inside fork-owned namespaces (_CD, _Impstation, _Mono, Corvax), and the Ungu species prototype I pulled directly (Resources/Prototypes/_Impstation/Species/ungu.yml, supplemental to the citation list) is fully self-contained, only referencing one shared generic upstream marking prototype rather than patching any upstream file — this matches the seed's zero-out-of-namespace finding, so 'fork-patches-upstream' is not supported. The assessor's own skip_because reasoning (hardsuit/clothing sprite layers, loadout/job wiring, markings, food/atmos balance) is about what a target codebase would have to add to actually make the species playable and equippable, not about the fork's own copy touching upstream files — i.e. integration-would-require-upstream-edits. None of the 10 cited evidence files themselves demonstrate that wiring cost (no clothing/sprite prototypes were among the citations), so this part of the claim rests on domain knowledge about SS14 species modding rather than the cited paths, but that read is sound and consistent with how new playable species are known to require touching existing clothing/job data to be usable, which is enough to keep this unit out of true drop-in territory.
  - unsupported: "shrimp-like Anomalocarids" — the Anomalocarid guidebook text (heat-vent biology, damage table) never describes their physical appearance as shrimp-like; that's an unverified visual gloss the assessor added, not something the cited file states
  - unsupported: "cat-people" for Tajaran — tajaran.yml is pure data (id/name/prototype/sounds fields) with no descriptive text; the feline framing is outside knowledge, not something the cited evidence file itself shows

### Species racial abilities

<a id="species-racial-abilities"></a>

Per-species active and passive abilities such as melee bonuses, hairballs, sprints that leave trails, mouth storage, gliding, blink teleports and reach limits for short species.

**sunrise**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 12 forks carry it*

Picking certain species gives you buttons nobody else has. A cat-person can crouch over a bleeding crewmate (or themselves) and lick the wound to slow the bleeding, but only on bare skin, so the patient has to be undressed first. Winged crew get a "Spread Wings" toggle and a "Take Flight" toggle that trades stamina for a big burst of speed. Small bird-like crew get a short-range dash they can fire off three times before it has to recharge, letting them cross a room or a gap almost instantly. Other odd trinkets sit on top: characters who can conjure a bar of soap, a cookie or a candy out of nowhere, dog-tinted or heart-tinted vision, and a set of flip/jump/dance/lie-down emotes with real animations that anyone can use.

- **Take** — Species stop being pure cosmetics and start playing differently — a flying or dashing crewmate changes chases, escapes and station traversal in a way a hair-and-tail reskin never does.
- **Skip** — The interesting half of it (flight, dash, licking) is written for Sunrise's own added species and hangs off their mob prototypes, so grafting means both patching your species/mob prototypes to grant the actions and re-balancing mobility abilities that let players skip the station layout your maps assume.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, security, service, movement
- **Needs** — [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species), [speech-accents-and-emotes](#speech-accents-and-emotes)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**starlight**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 12 forks carry it*

Playing a Shadekin, your character carries a "core" organ whose energy rises and falls with how dark the area around you is, shown as a five-step alert on your HUD (from pitch dark to dangerously bright) alongside an energy counter. Veteran Shadekin players who meet the requirements can roll as a Bright-eye: an otherworldly visitor who spends that energy to tear open a rift to "The Dark" on the station, blink onto someone to stun, blind and drag them into darkness, drop shadow snares that ensnare and stun whoever walks into them, and conjure a shade that follows them around. Their goals are to keep a rift open and to protect their core, so the crew's counterplay is to hunt for the core (which is worth a fortune to science and can't just be swapped into another Shadekin), flood the area with light, or use bluespace-crystal gear, goggles and anchors that destabilise the core and pin the Bright-eye out of nullspace. The same folder also adds smaller body-based quirks: an integrated gas-tank organ that gives a species its own internals toggle and tank readout instead of a backpack tank, a breath-holding effect, and a gnome curse that shrinks a victim, dresses them in a gnome outfit and replaces their scream with an endless giggle.

- **Take** — It turns a species pick into a genuine round-shaping threat with real crew counterplay (light, bluespace crystals, surgery for the core) rather than a cosmetic reskin, and the integrated gas-tank organ is a genuinely nicer internals experience for any breath-odd species.
- **Skip** — It is not a species ability pack you can lift a piece of: the Bright-eye antag needs the Shadekin species, the core organ, Starlight's nullspace dimension, a "The Dark" status effect, the Burned/Nightblind traits, playtime trackers and a pile of bespoke counterplay items, plus new maps/sprites, so the smallest honest graft is the whole subsystem and its lore.
- **Touches** — antagonist, science, medical, security, all-players
- **Needs** — [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species), [character-traits](#character-traits)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**impstation**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 12 forks carry it*

Each of Impstation's odd species gets its own button on the action bar, so picking a species is picking a way to play rather than just a sprite. A snail-person burns their thirst meter to sprint and leaves a slick trail of mucus behind them, a crab-like anomalocarid vents scalding steam out of its shell (and gets increasingly panicked "I'm burning from the inside out!" warnings if it waits too long) or spits a burst of water at someone, a bird-thing tears a chunk of flesh out of a downed victim and can rapidly rescramble its own face into a new one, a psychic can beam a cryptic vision into a stranger's head or pop a small animal from across the room, and a bull can charge forward into whoever it's aiming at. The mime gets a proper toolkit of mimed props that become real: invisible wall, chair, box and a three-tile blockade, all locked behind keeping the vow of silence (they can't even type on an AAC tablet). There are matching drawbacks too: very short characters can't reach a machine's console unless they're buckled into a chair, standing on a table or floating, some species physically can't two-hand a weapon, and hoofed species need special fat-handled tools to work at all.

- **Take** — It turns species choice into a real mechanical decision with both a signature toy and a signature handicap, and the individual pieces (a sprint that costs thirst, a heat vent, a charge, a mimed prop) are small self-contained abilities you can cherry-pick one at a time.
- **Skip** — Almost all of it is bolted to Impstation's own invented species roster, so grafting any single ability means deciding which of your species gets it and hand-wiring it into that species' prototypes; the drawback half (short-for-UI, can't-wield, cow tools) also gates upstream machine, wielding and tool interactions, which means balance complaints and edge-case bug reports on systems you didn't previously own.
- **Touches** — all-players, service, security, medical
- **Needs** — [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species), [assigned-roleplay-moods](#assigned-roleplay-moods)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Non-humanoid species pack

<a id="non-humanoid-species-pack"></a>

Playable species that cannot use the upstream humanoid rig and ship their own body plan: a bespoke body-part graph, a custom inventory template, a non-bipedal or taur sprite rig, or bespoke damage and atmospherics handling such as water-breathing vaporizers. Packs are indivisible: every species shipped in one vendored species directory takes this id in every fork that vendors it, including the originating fork. The Macrocosm pack (`Resources/Prototypes/_MACRO/Species/` = Decapoid, Gastropoid, Allulalo, Gray, Ant, Apid, plus their markings, textures and guidebook pages) is that case and is canonically here, not in `extra-playable-species`, even for the members that look humanoid.

**impstation, sunrise**

*loop-change · entangled · high confidence · 5 forks carry it*

These are playable species that break the normal human body plan, so picking one changes how you play every job for the whole round. An Impstation Decapoid is a crab: it survives vacuum and cold bare-skinned and scuttles fast in zero-g, but walks slowly under gravity, can't wear shoes, can't fire guns, and its huge left claw is a pry bar, cutter, melee weapon and raisable shield rather than a hand, so it effectively plays one-handed. It also can't speak or breathe without its irreplaceable vaporizer and vapor tank, which examine as full/low/bad/empty and need water from atmos when they run dry, and medics can't inject it at all because syringes won't pierce the shell, forcing pills and cryo instead. An Allulalo is a tiny fast saurian that stands on chairs to reach consoles, holds one item, and bites allies to dose them with a clotting adrenaline toxin; Apids fly permanently, hold one item, can never wear a backpack or jetpack, and arrive as cargo-purchased cryo pods that ghosts wake up in as full crew. Sunrise's half is quieter: xeno, predator and demon playables with their own body sprites and their own inventory windows (a xeno gets bra/pants slots and is blocked from hardsuits).

- **Take** — A Decapoid or Apid is a genuine self-imposed difficulty mode that reshapes an entire shift of play (one hand, no guns, no injections, a breathing consumable you must maintain) rather than being a reskin, and Impstation ships in-game guidebooks that already explain all of it to players.
- **Skip** — Every one of these species is a permanent tax on the rest of the codebase: each new hardsuit, syringe, backpack, chair-height console or hand-requiring interaction has to be re-checked against a one-handed, shoeless, unpierceable, wing-blocked body, and the vaporizer is an irreplaceable item whose loss is a hard round-ruiner that admins will be asked to fix.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, atmospherics, security, cargo, ghost-roles, science
- **Needs** — [character-markings-and-editor](#character-markings-and-editor)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

## Roleplay

### Server rules, space law and SOP guidebook

<a id="guidebook-rules-and-sop"></a>

Fork-authored in-game documentation covering server rules, space law, escalation policy, departmental standard operating procedure and onboarding guides.

**corvax, cosmatic-drift, funky, gaby-station-now-dumont-station, goobstation, harmony-station, impstation, monolith, rmc14, serbia-strong, sunrise, viva-station**

*loop-change · drop-in · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

Opening the in-game guidebook gives players a full shelf of server-written reference material instead of just upstream's mechanical guides: numbered server rules (Cosmatic Drift's Rule 1.0 through 3.9, Harmony's R0-R8 and S1-S3), a written space/corporate law codebook listing named offences with sentences, and a Standard Operating Procedure tree split department by department and often job by job (Funky Station has separate SOP pages for Captain, HoP, Warden, Detective, Chemist, Paramedic, Roboticist, Bartender, Mime, and so on, plus a page per alert level explaining what Green through Delta actually authorise). Security and Command can look up the charge or the procedure mid-round rather than arguing from memory, and a new player can read the server's actual expectations before their first shift. Viva Station goes further with a Command-only hidden SOP: the pages exist but are metashielded, and your character only "knows" them once you physically find a head of staff's issued guidebook, with higher-clearance books unlocking more of the document, which is what gives an RP-led riot something concrete to be leaking. Sunrise and RMC-14 attach onboarding on top of the text (a guided tutorial and new-player popups), which is a much larger build than the documentation itself.

- **Take** — It is pure content in fork-namespaced XML plus guideEntry prototypes, and it is the cheapest way to make Security and Command play to a codified law and procedure instead of improvising a different standard every shift.
- **Skip** — None of the text is yours: SOP, space law and numbered rules only work if they describe your server's actual policy, so grafting is really a rewrite of every page plus permanent upkeep as your rules drift, and nothing here is mechanically enforced.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, command, service, medical, engineering, cargo, science
- **Verified** — supported. Every checkable specific held up: CD's guides.ftl lists exactly Rule 1.0-3.9 plus per-department SOP and a crimes list; Harmony's guides.ftl lists exactly R0-R8 and S1-S3; Funky's sop.yml defines per-job guideEntry pages for every job named (Captain, HoP, Warden, Detective, Chemist, Paramedic, Roboticist, Bartender, Mime) plus Green-through-Delta alert pages whose XML (GreenAlert.xml) does spell out what is authorised; Corvax's CorporateLaw dir has numbered offence chapters plus Punishments.xml/CrimeList.xml; Viva's CommandHiddenStandardOperatingProcedure.xml states verbatim the metashield and the three clearance tiers unlocked by finding specific heads' guidebooks (worth knowing: enforcement is AHelp/admin convention, not a mechanic, which the claim's "metashielded" framing correctly implies); Sunrise has _Sunrise/Tutorial and RMC-14 has _RMC14/NewPlayer, backing the onboarding aside. Drop-in is confirmed at the mechanism level: upstream GuidebookWindow.GetSortedEntries computes roots as all entries ExceptWith children, so parentless fork-namespaced guideEntry prototypes (like Funky's SOP101) surface as top-level guidebook roots with zero upstream edits. One small practical trim: Funky's sop.yml references an OctarineAlert page and alert-level-octarine locale key from its cosmic-cult port, and Violet/Octarine alert levels are fork-specific, so a graft drops or rewrites those child entries — content pruning, not entanglement.

### Speech accents, verbs and emotes

<a id="speech-accents-and-emotes"></a>

Extra accents, speech verbs, emote prototypes, typing indicators and quick-phrase sets, including equipment- and species-driven voice filters, plus paired and animated social emotes. Everything here is prototype- and component-level chat decoration that runs entirely in-process. External speech synthesis (an API client, voice picker, job queue, cached audio) is `text-to-speech`, not this id; per-character bark sounds, which just play a shipped .ogg on speech, stay here.

**rmc14**

*activity · entangled · medium confidence · 14 forks carry it*

Typing gets quietly cleaned up: a filter rewrites out-of-character shorthand and meta-slang as you speak, so "surv" comes out as "survivor" and "OB" as "orbital bombardment", with separate word lists for the human side and the alien side. Socially, you can offer a fistbump, high five, handshake, hug or tail swipe to a specific person, who has about ten seconds to take it before you are visibly left hanging, and each one plays its own animation and sound. Pointing at something spawns a large arrow other people can see, with a differently coloured arrow when you point for your own squad, and different species scream, laugh and chirp with their own voice clips instead of a generic one. Radio prefixes are also policed so the alien hivemind channel only works for aliens and never for humans.

- **Take** — The paired hand emotes (offer, accept, or get left hanging) and the visible pointing arrows are genuine two-player social interactions rather than decoration, and they cost nothing balance-wise.
- **Skip** — The chat core is welded to RMC's own factions: the sanitiser word lists are all Aliens-franchise jargon, the prefix routing keys off their marine/xeno components, and the squad-coloured pointing arrow reaches into their squad system, so only the emote and species-voice slice travels cleanly and the rest needs rewriting for your setting.
- **Touches** — all-players, antagonist, command, security
- **Needs** — [squads-ranks-and-skills](#squads-ranks-and-skills), [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive)
- **Verified** — partly supported. Direct reading confirms the entanglement: RMCPointingComponent's squad-coloured arrow branch (RMCPointingSystem.cs) requires SquadMemberComponent from Content.Shared._RMC14.Marines.Squads, and SharedCMChatSystem gates the hivemind radio channel on MarineComponent/XenoComponent from the marine/xeno namespaces — exactly the squads-ranks-and-skills and xenomorph-hive dependencies claimed. The hand-emote system (fistbump/high-five/hug/handshake/tailswipe, 10s LeftHangingDelay, per-emote sound+effect, "left hanging" popup) and the pointing arrow both check out as real, self-contained mechanics. However the flagship chat-sanitizer example is factually backwards: the locale files show cm-chatsan-word-ob/orbital/orbital-bomb/orbital-bombardment all resolve cm-chatsan-replacement-* to "skyfire", not to "orbital bombardment" as the claim states — the filter turns real/meta terms into in-fiction alien slang, the opposite of what's described for that example.
  - unsupported: so "OB" as "orbital bombardment" — actual locale data (Resources/Locale/en-US/_RMC14/cm-chatsan.ftl) shows cm-chatsan-replacement-ob = "skyfire", and likewise cm-chatsan-replacement-orbital / -orbital-bomb / -orbital-bombardment all resolve to "skyfire", not "orbital bombardment". The claimed output text is wrong.

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 14 forks carry it*

Adds a social interactions panel: you target another player and pick from categorised gestures (Friendly, Face, Tail, Neck, Wings, Horn) such as a handshake, high-five, hug, sniff, cheek-pinch, face-pat or tail-stroke, each firing a randomly varied third-person line plus a sound effect and a short per-gesture cooldown. Which options appear depends on the situation: a tail-pat only shows if the target actually has a visible tail, licking someone's face requires your own mouth to be uncovered and only certain species can do it, so a mask or a helmet quietly removes options from your list. On top of that, players can write their own custom interactions (name, messages, icon, sound, cooldown), pop a floating cloud emote above their head with a chat command, and factions like xenos, blob, carp and zombies get their own colour-coded telepathic chat channel with a single-letter prefix; a profanity filter and anti-spam pass sit over normal speech.

- **Take** — It hands every player a rich, condition-aware social toolbox that makes downtime roleplay actually expressive, and the interaction prototypes are pure YAML so a fork can add its own gestures without touching code.
- **Skip** — The shipped interaction messages are hardcoded Russian strings in the prototypes rather than locale keys, so every gesture needs rewriting by hand, and the bundle drags in unrelated systems (chat sanitisation partials on upstream ChatSystem, a replacement headset radio UI, collective-mind channels tied to antag content) that a fork taking "emotes" does not necessarily want.
- **Touches** — all-players, roleplay, antagonist, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species), [clothing-and-wardrobe-pack](#clothing-and-wardrobe-pack)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**ephemeral-space**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 14 forks carry it*

Radio stops being a magic station-wide intercom: the further you are from the receiver, the more your transmission decays, with letters chewed into tildes and bursts of "--BZZT--" and "--KRSSH--" shoved between words, and past a hard range nobody hears you at all, so crews have to move toward relays or relay messages by hand. A hackable scrambler box smears that same static across every channel station-wide until someone spends fifteen seconds repairing it, and a scrambler examine tells you whether the processor is operational or malfunctioning. Alongside that, a wearable voice obfuscator makes you speak as "someone's muffled voice" (or as a monkey) instead of by name, a covert-message action pops a "Send Covert Message" dialog to push one line onto a chosen channel, a coughing affliction breaks your speech apart with hrgh/haughk/eugh interjections and cuts sentences short, and pain- and brain-damage-gated emotes like painslump and braingasp appear for hurt characters.

- **Take** — Distance-degraded radio plus a sabotageable station-wide scrambler turns comms into a resource crews fight over instead of a free omniscient channel, which is the single highest-leverage roleplay lever in this bundle.
- **Skip** — It is not the drop-in accent pack the label suggests: the falloff and distortion need hooks inside upstream's radio send/receive path, the channel gate reads a SenderWhitelist field the fork added to the upstream radio channel prototype, and nerfing global comms will make every department's coordination noticeably worse unless you also plan the relay placement and map work to compensate.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, antagonist, medical, engineering
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Player-authored documents and books

<a id="player-authored-text"></a>

Systems for player-written and printed text: form catalogs and document printers, signable paperwork, multi-page book readers and engraved inscriptions on items.

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

Adds a station copy machine that turns paperwork into a real activity. You walk up to it, browse a library of about 150 ready-made official forms sorted into department tabs (Central Command, Command, Security, Justice, Medical, Science, Engineering, Service, Supply, Prison, even a Syndicate set), pick one, and the machine spends a few seconds printing it while eating paper and ink from its own stocks. The printed sheet comes out already headed with the right departmental letterhead and auto-filled with the station name, the corporation name and the current time. There is also a slot you can feed an existing sheet into to run off duplicates of anything, including something you found or stole. Opening a printed form gives you a proper fillable document rather than a blank page: named blanks you click and type into, plus one-tap buttons that drop your character's real name into the signature line and your real job title into the position line, with a marked space at the bottom for stamps. So a lawyer can print a formal indictment, a doctor a death certificate or prescription authorization, the captain a certificate of commendation or a shuttle-call request, and the paper trail becomes something the crew actually passes around and stamps.

- **Take** — It gives every bureaucratic and command-adjacent role a concrete thing to do with a huge pre-written form library, turning "write a report" from a blank-page chore into a two-click printed document players will actually fill in and stamp.
- **Skip** — All 154 template documents are written in Russian markup and the visible strings live in Sunrise's own locale keys, so an English fork inherits a translation job roughly the size of the feature itself before a single form is readable; on top of that the fillable-field half hangs off upstream's PaperComponent/PaperWindow as partials, so it is not a clean drop-in, and the machine still needs map placement, a construction/lathe recipe and ink/paper resupply wired into your own station layouts.
- **Touches** — command, security, service, medical, cargo, science, engineering, all-players
- **Verified** — partly supported. Fetched all six cited files plus two more to settle the upstream question. categories.yml/templates.yml/pool_template.yml fully confirm the department tabs (Central Command x2, Command, Security, Justice, Medical, Science, Engineering, Service, Supply, Prison, Syndicate), the per-category letterhead header sprites, and ~140 real templates. CopyMachineComponent.cs confirms paper/ink consumption fields (PaperCost, IncCost), print duration, print sound, a CopySlot for feeding an existing sheet to duplicate it, and a Templates/TemplateCategoryGroupId filter list — matching the gameplay description closely. ApplicationEmployment.xml confirms {stationName}/{corpString}/{timeString} auto-fill tokens, [signature=]/[job=] fillable blanks, and a literal 'Место для печатей' (stamp placeholder) footer, and is written entirely in Russian, confirming the translation-burden claim. Crucially for graft_shape: PaperComponent.TemplateFields.cs lives under Content.Shared/_Sunrise/Paper/ but declares `namespace Content.Shared.Paper` and re-declares `PaperBoundUserInterfaceState` with extra fields (ImageContent, ImageScale, TemplateFieldsEnabled). I fetched Content.Shared/Paper/PaperComponent.cs (the plain, non-_Sunrise path) from this fork and compared it against the real upstream space-wizards file: upstream defines PaperBoundUserInterfaceState directly inside PaperComponent.cs, but this fork's copy of that same upstream-path file has had that nested class deleted so the _Sunrise partial can redefine it with more fields. That is a genuine in-place edit of an upstream-path file, not just a hypothetical integration cost — so upstream_reading is 'both': the fork's own tree already patches an upstream file, and any target codebase would need the equivalent edit to avoid a duplicate-type build break. This directly validates the seed's warning that path-only data can't see in-place upstream edits, and confirms (rather than overturns) the claim's own 'needs-upstream-edits' verdict.
  - unsupported: "you browse a library of about 150 ready-made official forms" — templates.yml contains 154 docTemplate blocks but 13 of those are abstract:true category-header templates (not user-selectable forms), leaving 141 actual templates; the pool actually wired into the copy machine (pool_template.yml, pool id "Sunrise") lists exactly 140 template IDs. The real number is ~140, not ~150.

**corvax**

*activity · drop-in · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

A document printer sits in offices as a paper-fed machine: you feed it blank paper, slot your ID card into it, and pick from a menu of about sixty ready-made Nanotrasen forms — situation reports, employment and dismissal statements, access and weapon-carry permits, search warrants, death and marriage certificates, indictments and sentences, euthanasia and shuttle requests. The printed sheet comes out already filled in with your name, your job title, the station name and the current station time, so you only write the actual content and stamp it. It also ships department stamps for the Internal Affairs Agent and the Magistrate, a legal-department pen, and a shelf of in-universe lore books players can read. Emagging the printer unlocks a handful of joke/hostile forms like liquidation notices and a declaration of military action.

- **Take** — It turns "write a form on blank paper" roleplay into a one-click, pre-filled bureaucratic workflow, giving lawyers, IAAs, command and clerks a real prop library instead of freehand typing.
- **Skip** — Every one of the ~60 form bodies and all the lore books exist only as Russian locale strings — en-US has just the four substitution tags — so grafting it means writing sixty documents of English bureaucratic prose yourself and then maintaining them.
- **Touches** — command, security, service, all-players
- **Verified** — supported; assessed as needs upstream edits, corrected by the verifier. Every gameplay specific checks out: printer.yml has MaterialStorage(Document tag)+ItemSlots(IdCard), DocumentPrinterSystem.cs substitutes doc-var-date/station/name/job exactly as described, CorvaxDocumentsPack has 59 recipes (matches "about sixty") spanning reports/certificates/permits/indictments named as claimed, CorvaxEmagDocumentsPack has 5 joke recipes including NoticeOfLiquidation and NoteBeginningMilitaryActions, rubber_stamp.yml has RubberStampIAA and RubberStampMagistrate, pen.yml has PenLegal, books.yml has a dozen+ BookLoreBase lore entries. The skip_because claim is exactly confirmed: en-US/corvax/paper/ contains only doc-printer-tags.ftl (4 tag lines), while ru-RU/corvax/paper/ has the real doc-printer.ftl, books-lore.ftl, book-busido.ftl, book-rulebook.ftl content files. Where the claim is shakier is graft_shape: every file I found (Content.Shared/Corvax/Documents/DocumentPrinterComponent.cs, Content.Server/Corvax/Documents/DocumentPrinterSystem.cs, all the Resources/Prototypes/Corvax/* yml, Resources/Locale/*/corvax/paper/*) sits inside Corvax-namespaced paths with zero upstream-directory files touched, and I found no evidence that wiring it into another codebase requires editing any upstream file (it just references generic BaseLathe/Paper/IdCard/ItemSlots APIs). The claim's own justification for "needs-upstream-edits" is a translation/content-authoring burden (60 documents untranslated), not a code-integration dependency on upstream files, so it conflates two different senses of "graft cost." Structurally this reads as drop-in with a large content-completeness caveat, not as needs-upstream-edits in the taxonomy's own terms.

**forky**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 11 forks carry it*

Stations get a bureaucracy machine: an XF-880 document printer you can build and click open, giving a searchable, icon-tabbed catalogue of official station forms sorted by department (Administrative, Legal, Engineering, Medical, Logistics, R&D, Internal Affairs, Miscellaneous). Pick a form (promotion notice, job transfer, demotion notice, NDA and so on) and it prints a pre-written paper you then fill in and sign; locked forms show a "Requires: Command" tooltip so only the right ID can print them. It can also copy a paper you feed into its tray, it jams roughly one print in thirty until someone opens the maintenance panel and clears it, and it has wires: cut the manager wire and a hidden "Unregulated" tab appears with joke contraband paperwork (an XO murder authorisation, a clown all-access form), while emagging it unlocks a scrambled-name Syndicate tab with recruitment paperwork. Separately, a fax machine carried inside a backpack now drops received papers straight into that bag or the carrier's hands instead of onto the floor, so a pocket fax actually works on the move, including when something gets faxecuted.

- **Take** — It turns paperwork roleplay from "type it yourself in a blank sheet" into a real, discoverable station machine, with a wire/emag secret tab that gives Sec and antags a reason to care about the office printer.
- **Skip** — Every form is only as good as its written text, so the value is a content library you have to keep writing and translating; the machine also needs its own sprites, circuitboard, wire layout and map placement in each office before anyone finds it.
- **Touches** — command, service, security, medical, engineering, cargo, science, antagonist
- **Verified** — supported. Every specific gameplay detail checks out against the actual files: the XF-880 name, the exact category list/order (Administrative, Legal, Engineering, Medical, Logistics, R&D, Internal Affairs, Miscellaneous), the jamOneInChance:30 (~1-in-30 jam), the "Requires: {$access}" locked tooltip tied to `access: Command` fields, the manager-wire-cut Unregulated tab with DocXOMurder/DocClownAAForm, the emag-revealed scrambled-name Syndicate tab with DocSyndicateRecruitment, and HandheldFaxSystem.cs's backpack/hands paper redirection plus its FaxecuteFiringEvent hook. More importantly, the claim's own graft_shape ("needs-upstream-edits") is not just correct but understated: diffing FaxSystem.cs against the space-wizards upstream baseline shows the fork's copy of the upstream file Content.Server/Fax/FaxSystem.cs was directly edited in place (a `using Content.Shared._Funkystation.Fax;` import plus a `// _Funkystation:` comment raising the fork-only `FaxPaperPrintedEvent` that does not exist in vanilla FaxSystem.cs) to let HandheldFaxSystem hook in. That means this is fork-patches-upstream, not merely integration-would-require-upstream-edits — the seed's 'drop-in' guess is refuted outright, and the assessor's downgrade to needs-upstream-edits is confirmed and actually conservative.

### Ghost roles, respawn and game-master roles

<a id="gamemaster-and-ghost-roles"></a>

Ways for dead or waiting players to re-enter the round: extra ghost roles, respawn paths after a cooldown, and non-embodied roles that steer the shift from behind the scenes.

**ephemeral-space**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Players who are watching rather than playing can join a round as a "stagehand", a floating theatergoer with a randomised face who drifts through the station, sees in the dark, listens in on every radio channel, and can examine anyone but touch nothing. An Observe window lists everyone currently alive with their name, job and objectives, and a Warp button snaps you into following that person. Backstage you get a running feed of the shift's drama (who killed whom, who committed suicide, whose objective just succeeded or failed, who slipped into a new secret identity, who else joined the crew) with the bigger news printed in bigger text. Two actions on long cooldowns let the audience Cheer or Boo, which plays crowd noise and can carry through to the living crew, who get a nagging "I think the performers might have heard that one..." Separately, dying puts you through a short cutscene and then ghosts you automatically after about fifteen seconds instead of leaving you lying in your body waiting.

- **Take** — It turns dead and waiting players from an idle ghost crowd into an engaged audience with a curated drama feed, a warp-to-anyone spectator menu, and a way to react audibly into the live round.
- **Skip** — The bundle is not just the spectator role: the auto-ghost half forcibly severs players from their bodies fifteen seconds after death with return-to-body disabled, which cuts across defib and cloning play, and the stagehand mob and join gate both reach into other Ephemeral Space systems (lobby theatergoer marker, voting, interaction blocking, death cutscene, viewcone) that would have to be stubbed or ported alongside it.
- **Touches** — ghost-roles, all-players, medical, antagonist
- **Needs** — [lobby-and-character-ui](#lobby-and-character-ui), [secret-identities](#secret-identities)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

When you press the ghost key you now get a confirmation box asking whether you really want to leave your body, so nobody spectates by accident. Once you are a ghost, the old plain warp list is replaced with a searchable panel: locations on one side, living players sorted by department and job with clear markers for who is dead, who has gone AFK, and who is already a ghost, plus a separate tab that lists the round's current antagonists so you can jump straight to the interesting fight instead of hunting for it. Dead players also get somewhere to go: a self-contained off-station bar with drinks, an arcade, a garden and animals, where ghosts can hang out and chat instead of drifting through walls. On top of that there are extra reinforcement slots ghosts can claim mid-round, joining a pirate boarding crew or a syndicate agent team as human, reptilian, dwarf, vox, vulpkanin or felinid.

- **Take** — It fixes the deadest part of the round: the ghost panel makes spectating actually navigable (antagonist tab, department grouping, AFK/dead markers) and the ghost bar gives dead players somewhere to socialise instead of quietly disconnecting.
- **Skip** — The ghost panel is a wholesale replacement of upstream's warp window and hangs off a partial of upstream's GhostSystem, so every upstream ghost-UI change becomes a merge conflict you own forever; the ghost bar is a 20k-line map you also have to maintain, and its comments and doc strings are in Russian.
- **Touches** — ghost-roles, all-players, antagonist
- **Needs** — [pirate-crew](#pirate-crew), [nuclear-operatives](#nuclear-operatives), [extra-playable-species](#extra-playable-species)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*structural · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Dying stops being the end of your shift. As a ghost you get a panel listing every body your character has ever inhabited, each labelled as still available, abandoned, currently used by someone else, in cryo, or destroyed, and you can hop back into one you left behind or write it off for good. If nothing is left to return to, a fifteen minute countdown ticks in the ghost window and then hands you a respawn button that puts you back into the round. Ghosts also get a checkbox list of radio channels so they can mute the departments they do not care about, an option to hide the ghost sprite down to just the hat, and a toggle for seeing security HUD icons. Admins get click-a-target actions that turn any mob into an open ghost role on the spot (with a window to set its name, description and rules) or strip that role away again, and a set of station pet spawners (Runtime, Floppa, Bingus, Bandito and friends) that let waiting players take over the cats.

- **Take** — It answers the single biggest dead-player complaint at once: a timed respawn path plus a browsable list of your old bodies means an early death is a fifteen minute wait instead of spectating for an hour.
- **Skip** — Free respawn plus body-hopping rewrites the stakes of death for medical, security and antagonists alike, and the code carries an SS220 CLA with a hosting restriction rather than a plain open licence, so it is not a clean legal lift.
- **Touches** — ghost-roles, all-players, admin/gamemaster, command
- **Verified** — supported. Every specific gameplay detail checks out against the actual code, not just the cited evidence: BodyStateToEnter enum (Available/Abandoned/Engaged/InCryo/Destroyed) matches the claimed body-list labels exactly, and I found and read the uncited GhostBodyCard.xaml.cs which renders those five states verbatim with distinct colors and follow/return/delete buttons ("hop back in" / "write it off"). MindExtensionData.RespawnTime = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(15) and MindRespawnSystem's SetRespawnAvailable logic confirm the 15-minute countdown gated on "nothing left to return to." GhostHearingWindow.xaml.cs confirms the radio-channel checkbox list. Ghost.yml's ActionToggleAGhostBodyVisuals ("Скрыть тело... оставив только шляпу" = hide body, leaving only the hat) and ActionToggleHudOnOther (toggle SB-visor vision) match those two toggles precisely. GhostRoleCast.yml's three EntityTargetActions (cast/remove/settings for name+description+rules) exactly match the admin click-a-target claim. ghost_role_pet_spawner.yml/ghost_role_pets.yml confirm Runtime, Floppa, Bandito, Bingus (plus Exception, McGriff as "friends") as GhostRole+GhostTakeoverAvailable mobs. On graft shape: all 11 cited files plus every additional file I found by browsing (Content.Client/Server/SS220/MindExtension, GhostAdditionUIController.cs) live strictly under /SS220/ namespace paths, so 'fork-patches-upstream' is false — the fork's copy is self-contained. But GhostAdditionUIController hooks into GameplayStateLoadController.OnScreenLoad and the admin target-actions would need to be granted through whatever admin-rank/verb config exists in a receiving codebase, and the pet ghost-roles reference parent mob prototypes (MobCatRuntime etc.) that must already exist — so a real integration into another fork's codebase plausibly needs upstream-file edits even though the SS220 fork's own tree never touches upstream files itself. The claim's stated 'needs-upstream-edits' reasoning (design-impact on death stakes, SS220 CLA hosting restriction) is more about game-design/legal risk than code friction, but the classification still lands correctly for the integration-edits reading.

**backmen**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Dead players stop being stuck as ghosts: after a wait timer (and only while the server is under a population cap) a ghost can open a menu, pick one of their saved characters and a station, and walk back on as fresh crew in whatever job still has an open slot, with characters they already played that round excluded. Alongside that, command can use a reinforcement console to type a short brief and request one to three specific job roles, which goes out as an offer waiting ghosts can claim and arrive to fill. Admins can also call in whole response teams (standard ERT, Alpha/Epsilon/Tay variants, a blob-response squad, a Death Squad) that arrive on their own shuttle with a station-wide announcement and alarm sound, with the number of slots scaling to the player count and ghosts raffling for them. There are extra standalone ghost roles too (maintenance drone, a "canned clown" that pops out to help the clown, an InteQ mercenary garrison running a lavaland prison facility), supply pods that get called down with a targeting hologram and a falling-pod animation before landing (some carrying a person rather than loot), and cosmetic ghost sprite skins (angel, pony, astronaut) for sponsors.

- **Take** — Ghost respawn plus a command-driven reinforcement request is the single biggest fix for the "died at 20 minutes, nothing to do for the next hour" problem, and it keeps mid-round population up on long shifts.
- **Skip** — It is a bundle, not a feature: the response teams need Backmen's own shuttle maps, blob, and lavaland to exist; the ghost skins need a sponsor system; and the respawn path reaches into the game ticker, job slots, preferences and playtime tracking, so you would be cherry-picking one strand and rebuilding the rest yourself.
- **Touches** — ghost-roles, all-players, command, security, antagonist, admin
- **Needs** — [blob](#blob), [lavaland-mining-planet](#lavaland-mining-planet), [sponsor-and-donator-perks](#sponsor-and-donator-perks)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.
