### Vision overlays and visors

<a id="screen-overlays-and-visors"></a>

Wearable and ambient post-processing that changes what the player can see: tinted goggles and welding visors, night vision, motion detectors, CRT and grain filters, and limited-vision cones.

**rmc14**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Players get gear that changes what they can actually see. A powered night-vision visor flips down over the helmet and lifts the darkness (either partially or fully, draining a power cell while it runs, and examining the helmet tells you which visor is down and how much charge is left; a screwdriver pops visors back out). A handheld motion detector is toggled on and pings on a timer, painting moving contacts as blips around you out to about seven tiles on short range or fourteen on long, with an audible sweep tone and a different tone when nothing is out there, and it shuts itself off if you drop it. Binocular-style eyewear has a toggle action that pushes your view slightly further out so you can spot things before they reach you, and a rangefinder window reports coordinates on a target. Squad leaders get a tracker readout listing their fireteam and where each member is, plus a security-camera viewer window for watching remote feeds.

- **Take** — Motion detection, night vision and the visor-swap loop turn darkness and line of sight into things players actively manage with gear, which is a real tactical layer SS14 does not otherwise have.
- **Skip** — It is written for RMC-14's Colonial Marines total conversion: night vision branches on xeno-specific components, the trackers are a table of CM job ids and squad alerts, and the visor logic assumes the fork's helmet-container gear, so almost none of it lands without dragging squads, jobs and the xeno side along.
- **Touches** — security, command, antagonist, all-players
- **Needs** — [squads-ranks-and-skills](#squads-ranks-and-skills), [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive), [extra-job-roster](#extra-job-roster)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

This is a grab-bag of things that change what your screen shows you. The heavy hitters are sight modes: a thermal mode (from goggles, worn clothing or an implant) that picks out warm bodies through darkness and walls out to a set radius, a night-vision mode that bathes everything around you in a soft tinted glow so dark maintenance reads clearly, and a Tajaran-style "Activate Keen Hearing" button that briefly outlines your surroundings for about five seconds on a two-minute cooldown. The other half is atmosphere and mischief: lamps and light sources get soft bloom halos, wall corners get smoothed so the vision cone edges look less blocky, and a hallucination effect makes you periodically see creatures and objects that other players cannot, unless you are wearing the item that protects against that particular hallucination. Screen shaders for glitching, blurring and fading are included, plus a small overlay that labels items with their cargo sell price.

- **Take** — Seeing warm bodies through walls and darkness is the single biggest change you can make to how hunting, hiding and searching feel, and this ships it in three flavours (goggle, implant, racial) off one shared overlay stack that other vision effects can plug into later.
- **Skip** — Every file carries the SS220 CLA/EULA with an explicit hosting restriction, and some of it is relicensed downstream of CM-14 and Imperial code, so it is not cleanly compatible with an AGPL fork; on top of that the overlay work reaches into upstream draw-depth ordering and needs its own .swsl shader and mask assets brought along.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, antagonist, cargo, science
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**stellar-station**

*cosmetic · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

The station stops looking flat. Walls are drawn as tall, standing structures rather than plain tiles, and when you walk behind one the part blocking your view fades out so you can still see your character and the room around you. Characters and objects cast soft drop shadows on the floor, and the whole screen runs through a post-processing pass that adds a gentle vignette toward the edges, brighter bloom around light sources, and a colour grade that can be swapped per situation, with a shockwave ripple effect for big impacts. Players who dislike it can switch the post-processing off in their own settings.

- **Take** — It is the single biggest change to how the game looks — tall walls that cut away, shadows and screen grading make the station read as a place rather than a tile grid, and it costs nothing in round balance.
- **Skip** — The cutaway renderer is wired into the fork's own wall-smoothing/wall-rendering rework and cannot be lifted alone, and the post-process shader carries an explicit all-rights-reserved CrystallEdge notice with usage granted only to Stellar Station, so that piece is not yours to take.
- **Touches** — all-players
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Storage and inventory rework

<a id="storage-and-inventory-rework"></a>

Changes how carried gear is organised: nested pouches and rigs, slot-blocking clothing, extendable equipment slots and reworked pickup and hands flow. Requires a code-level change to storage or inventory behaviour; a `Textures/.../Interface/` subdirectory alone is interface art, not a storage rework.

**cosmatic-drift, goobstation, rmc14, serbia-strong, starlight, sunrise**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 8 forks carry it*

This is a grab-bag of changes to where players can put things and who can pick them up. You get clothing that unpacks a whole outfit when you put it on (a death squad hat that dresses you in the matching jumpsuit, hardsuit, mask, belt and PDA), and clothing that blocks other slots while worn, so a bulky item stops you equipping anything on the slots it covers. Storage gains flavours: a bag or crate only its owner can open instantly (anyone else stands there for about five seconds with a popup announcing they are rummaging), containers you can take from but never put into, a mouth slot you can swallow an item into and involuntarily spit out when hit hard enough, hidden compartments you unscrew open with a screwdriver, a concealed slot inside your own hand toggled by an action, and dumping ore straight from your satchel into a machine without emptying it first. Items can also refuse to be picked up again for a few seconds after being set down (so a felinid dropped on the floor gets a head start to run), refuse to be worn by the wrong species entirely, and species like mice, borgs, arachnids and terminators get their own slot layouts instead of the human one. RMC-14 goes furthest, with military pouches and rigs, nested storage inside storage, quick-draw holsters, and a pickup and hands flow where you drop what you are holding when you go down.

- **Take** — Several of these are small, self-contained quality-of-life pieces (slot-blocking clothing, read-only storage, re-pickup cooldown, species clothing whitelists) that each solve a real friction point and can be lifted one at a time without taking the whole rework.
- **Skip** — Storage, hands and inventory are the most-touched systems in the game and the deeper pieces (nested pouches, predicted hands, custom inventory templates) hook into upstream code that churns constantly, so every upstream sync will drag conflicts through the parts of the codebase players notice fastest when they break.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, cargo, medical, antagonist, extra-playable-species
- **Verified** — partly supported. I re-fetched all 14 cited files (cosmatic-drift, starlight, sunrise, goobstation, and the SS220-namespaced files which live in the serbia-strong repo). Every one exists exactly as described and each lives inside a fork-owned namespace directory (_CD, _Starlight, _Sunrise, Goobstation.Shared, or Content.Shared.SS220) — I found no evidence any fork's own copy patches an upstream-owned file, so this is not 'fork-patches-upstream'. But the feature set (ExtendableClothing unpacking a full loadout, ClothingBlockSlotsOnEquip, per-species Inventory templateId/speciesId, ClothingWhitelist, ReadOnlyStorage) only does anything once it is attached to specific upstream clothing/species/storage prototypes that live outside these namespaces, so wiring it into another codebase means touching upstream prototype files even though the fork's own code is self-contained — that is 'integration-would-require-upstream-edits', matching the claim's downgrade from the drop-in seed. Separately worth flagging: the SS220-namespaced files (InnerHandToggleableComponent, ToggleableItemSlotComponent, TransferMaterialsInStorageComponent) carry a header citing a custom SS220 CLA/EULA "with a hosting restriction", a practical grafting obstacle independent of the code-shape question.
  - unsupported: "RMC-14 goes furthest, with military pouches and rigs, nested storage inside storage, quick-draw holsters, and a pickup and hands flow where you drop what you are holding when you go down." — zero RMC-14 paths appear anywhere in evidence_cited despite rmc14 being one of the six listed sources; this entire paragraph is unverifiable from the cited evidence.
  - unsupported: "species like mice, borgs, arachnids and terminators get their own slot layouts instead of the human one" — the cited file (starlight inventories.yml) only defines speciesId/templateId entries for arachnid, avali, cyclorite, felionoid, moth, reptilian, resomi, vox, vulpkanin, lagomorph, rodentia, diona, thaven. There is no mouse/mice, borg, or terminator entry anywhere in that 662-line file. Only 'arachnid' of the four named examples is actually confirmed.

### PDA chat messenger

<a id="nanochat-messenger"></a>

Turns handhelds into a phone-style chat network with number lookup, per-contact threads, group chats, emoji and delivery logs.

**funky**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every crewmember's PDA becomes a phone: you get your own number, look people up by name or number, and hold private one-to-one text threads that show whether a message actually got delivered, with per-chat mute and the option to hide your number from the directory. Funky's layer on top adds named group chats — you create one, invite people by searching the crew directory, view the member list, promote others to admin, kick people out or leave yourself, with the creator marked as owner. Messages can carry picture emoji picked from a searchable, categorised picker (meme faces, character emotes) that render inline in the conversation rather than as text.

- **Take** — Gives the whole crew a private, non-radio communication channel with group coordination, which changes how departments organise without touching any combat or engineering balance.
- **Skip** — Funky's group-chat and emoji work is stitched into the vendored Delta-V NanoChat cartridge files with inline "Funky Station - Group Chats" markers rather than living in fork-only files, so you must first port the whole Delta-V messenger and then re-resolve those same shared/server/UI files on every future sync; it also hands antagonists an untraceable private group channel outside radio.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, service, antagonist
- **Needs** — [pda-cartridge-programs](#pda-cartridge-programs)
- **Verified** — supported. Every gameplay specific maps to a fetched file: DeliveryFailed, MutedChats, ListNumber, group-chat enum members and Owner/Admin fields all carrying inline "Funky Station - Group Chats" markers inside vendored _DV files, plus categorised/searchable emoji prototypes rendered inline by an EmojiTag markup handler. The needs-upstream-edits downgrade is correct in both senses: code search shows the Funky markers live in four _DV files (not _Funkystation), and the fork's copy of the true upstream Resources/Prototypes/Entities/Objects/Devices/pda.yml is edited in place to add NanoChatCartridge to crew PDA program lists.

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every crewmate's PDA gains a messenger app that behaves like a phone chat client: a searchable contact list of everyone else who has a PDA, one-on-one message threads, and group chats you can create, name, invite people into, accept or decline invites for, promote/remove members in, mute, or leave. You get popup notifications for new messages and invites, can delete your own messages, and can drop in animated emoji from a picker with recent and favourite tabs. Everyone is auto-enrolled at round start into a station-wide chat plus a chat for their own department, so a doctor lands in Medical and Common without setting anything up. Between real conversations your PDA also buzzes with junk mail from fake senders (casino ads, dating spam, Nigerian-prince scams, fake emergency alerts), tuned by server settings for how often it arrives and how many players get hit. The whole network runs off a physical messenger server machine on the station, and the app shows a "Server unavailable" banner when that machine is not reachable.

- **Take** — It gives every player a private, persistent, department-aware text channel that radio cannot do (quiet one-to-one talk, ad-hoc groups, scrollback), and it is one of the highest-adoption fork features because it fills an obvious hole in station comms.
- **Skip** — A frictionless private chat for everyone quietly weakens radio, headset-stealing and comms-sabotage play, and the package is much bigger than "a chat window": a station server machine that has to be mapped in, spam datasets, a full emoji sprite sheet and picker, and a hard-coded auto-group table keyed to your department and radio-channel prototype ids that you must re-point at a fork roster.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, medical, engineering, cargo, science, service, antagonist
- **Verified** — partly supported. Upstream Resources/Prototypes/Entities/Objects/Devices/pda.yml in the fork carries Sunrise-Start/End blocks adding MessengerCartridge to PDA preinstalled programs (lines 89-94, 172-177, 192-197), so the fork really does edit upstream files in place, which the path-data seed could not see. Every other substantive gameplay claim (auto_groups.yml common+department enrollment, spam categories and cvar tuning in MessengerServerSystem.Spam.cs, delete-own-message check, 265 animated emoji states, "Server unavailable" locale string) checked out except the nonexistent member-promote action.
  - unsupported: "promote/remove members in" — remove is real (MessengerServerSystem.Groups.cs, owner-gated), but there is no promote or ownership-transfer command anywhere in the messenger server code; the group owner is fixed at creation

**cosmatic-drift**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Every crewmember's ID gets a four-digit phone number, and opening NanoChat on their PDA gives them a phone-style messenger: a contacts list down one side, a conversation thread with typed message bubbles on the other, and a popup notification when someone texts them (mutable if you want quiet). You add someone by punching in their number and a name, or by browsing the station directory of everyone who chose to list themselves, though the lookup and the sending both die if telecomms is down or you are off-station, and the message comes back marked as failed to deliver. Because the history lives on the ID card itself, Security can jam a confiscated card into a log probe and read out every message sent and received, and a card thrown in a microwave has its history wiped or scrambled into nonsense.

- **Take** — Private one-to-one text alongside public radio is a genuinely new social channel, and tying the history to the ID card gives Security real forensic evidence and gives antags a real reason to destroy a card.
- **Skip** — It is a large bespoke UI (seven client widgets) that also has to reach into upstream ID cards, PDAs, the log probe and microwave interactions, so it is not a clean drop-in, and once crew can text privately a lot of chatter leaves the radio channels your other content assumes people are listening to.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, command, antagonist, service
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched all five cited files plus the LogProbeCartridgeSystem partial, the NanoChatSystem microwave handler, identification_cards.yml, and name_identifier_groups.yml directly from the cosmatic-drift repo. Every substantive gameplay claim checks out: 4-digit numbers (nameIdentifierGroup maxValue 9999), contacts by manual entry or station-directory lookup gated on ListNumber, telecomms/off-station send failure (HasActiveServer + station-match checks), log-probe forensic read of a scanned card's Messages/Recipients, and microwave erase (10%) or character-scramble-with-possible-recipient-reassignment. The drop-in seed is correctly downgraded: Content.Server/CartridgeLoader/Cartridges/LogProbeCartridgeSystem.cs (upstream path, no _CD) is edited in place with '// CD' markers calling InitializeNanoChat() and threading ScannedNanoChatData through LogProbeUiState's constructor, and Resources/Prototypes/Entities/Objects/Misc/identification_cards.yml (upstream path) has '- type: NanoChatCard # CD' appended to essentially every ID card prototype (crew, ERT, deathsquad, CBURN, nuke-ops). That is the fork's own copy patching upstream files, not merely a hypothetical integration cost — so 'both' readings of upstream_reading are literally true here: the fork's copy already touches upstream files, and grafting into a different tree would still require adding the equivalent component/prototype lines and partial-class hook yourself. Minor overclaim on exact widget count aside, this is a solid, well-evidenced 'needs-upstream-edits' call.
  - unsupported: "It is a large bespoke UI (seven client widgets)" — the actual Content.Client/_CD/CartridgeLoader/Cartridges/ directory has 5 XAML widgets (NanoChatEntry, NanoChatLogEntry, NanoChatLookupView, NanoChatMessageBubble, NanoChatUiFragment) plus one non-XAML UI-fragment handler class (NanoChatUi.cs) — 6 files total, not 7.

**gaby-station-now-dumont-station**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Crew carry a chat card in their PDA that works like a phone: you look people up by name or number, open a thread with them, and text back and forth in real time from anywhere on the station. This fork's version adds group chats — you name a group, invite people by searching name or number, and the owner or an admin can promote, demote, or kick members, while anyone can leave. Messages can carry picture emoji picked from a searchable tray sorted into categories (reactions, faces, food, department badges, robots, plushies, memes), and you can mute a single noisy conversation without silencing the rest. Message history is physical: examining a card shows its number, and microwaving one wipes or scrambles everything on it, so a card can be destroyed as evidence.

- **Take** — It gives the whole crew a private, person-to-person and group text channel that radio does not cover, and the group/emoji/mute layer is what makes people actually use it instead of shouting on comms.
- **Skip** — The fork's emoji layer sits on top of a whole vendored messenger stack from two other forks (Delta-V's NanoChat plus Funkystation's expansion) rather than on upstream, so you inherit three namespaces of code plus a large sprite library, and this fork's own emoji prototype ids are written in Portuguese, which you would have to relabel or live with.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, service
- **Verified** — partly supported. Direct fetches confirm the group-chat/emoji/mute/microwave mechanics in the _Funkystation and _DV files, and code search plus raw fetches prove the fork's own copy patches upstream files (pda.yml preinstalls NanoChatCartridge, identification_cards.yml adds NanoChatCard, AgentIDCardSystem.cs imports _DV.NanoChat), so needs-upstream-edits stands in the fork-patches-upstream sense. Two specifics fail verification: admins cannot demote (creator-only per GroupMembersPopup.xaml.cs), and the emoji category list includes a nonexistent "faces" category.
  - unsupported: "the owner or an admin can promote, demote, or kick members" — GroupMembersPopup.xaml.cs explicitly disables demote for non-creators (line 94: if (!isCreator && canDeadmin) canDeadmin = false), so admins can promote and kick but only the owner can demote
  - unsupported: "sorted into categories (reactions, faces, food, department badges, robots, plushies, memes)" — there is no "faces" category in emojis.yml; the real categories are Reacoes, Simbolos, Acoes, Memes, Cabecas (heads), Comida, Departamentos, Misc, Plushies, Robos
  - unsupported: "you inherit three namespaces of code" — NanoChat files span five namespace directories (_DV, _Funkystation, _Gabystation, _Dumont, _Andromeda), and the cited _Gabystation NanoChatUiMessageEvent.cs actually contains NanoBank (bank app) code, not messenger code

**deltav**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 6 forks carry it*

Your PDA gains a NanoChat app that works like a phone: your ID card is assigned a number (visible when you examine it), and you can look up other crew's listed numbers through the station telecomms server, add them as named contacts with a job title, and hold a private one-to-one text conversation with each. New messages pop up as a notification naming the sender and their job, you can mute individual chats or all notifications, unlist your own number so nobody can find you, and a message that cannot reach its target comes back marked as failed to deliver. The paper trail is real: a security log probe can dump another card's stored conversations and known contacts, and microwaving a card wipes or scrambles its message history.

- **Take** — It gives every player private, targeted, off-radio communication with a real evidence trail, which creates secrecy, blackmail and investigation play that public radio channels cannot.
- **Skip** — Private untraceable-feeling chat pulls conversation out of the shared radio space and into invisible one-to-one threads, which quietly reduces the ambient chatter other players get to overhear and react to; it also needs the messages stored on ID cards, the app preinstalled on PDAs and the log probe extended, so it is not a clean bolt-on.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, command, antagonist, service
- **Verified** — supported. Every specific gameplay claim (examine number, telecomms lookup, unlisting, job-titled contacts, sender+job notifications, per-chat and global mute, failed-to-deliver, log-probe dump of messages and contacts, microwave wipe/scramble) maps to a concrete locale string or NanoChatCardComponent field I read. The fork's own tree edits upstream files in place: LogProbeCartridgeSystem.cs in Content.Server/CartridgeLoader/Cartridges has DeltaV NanoChat-scanning insertions, and upstream pda.yml preinstalls NanoChatCartridge, so needs-upstream-edits is correct in the fork-patches-upstream sense.

### PDA cartridge programs

<a id="pda-cartridge-programs"></a>

Extra software for handheld computers: security watchlists, stock trading, crime assistants, banking apps, news readers and logistics dashboards.

**backmen, deltav, gaby-station-now-dumont-station, sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 8 forks carry it*

These forks fill the PDA's program slot with extra apps you slot in like a cartridge and tap open on your handheld. A security officer opens SecWatch to see a live list of who is currently wanted and why ("Everything's calm. Why not enjoy a Monkin Donut?" when nobody is), or runs Crime Assist, a yes/no questionnaire that walks you through "was the victim an employee?", "did they die?", "was it intentional?" and spits out a numbered charge like "Code 301: Manslaughter" with the legal definition and suggested punishment, so a green officer can book someone correctly without a lawyer. Other apps: a bank/NanoBank app showing your account number, owner and balance and pinging you when money moves; StockTrading, a market screen where you buy and sell shares in galactic companies with a price-history graph and your spesos balance; MailMetrics, a courier scoreboard tracking earnings from delivered mail against losses from expired, damaged and tampered parcels with a success-rate percentage; and a glimmer monitor charting the station's psionic reading in real time. Sunrise adds a corporate law reference reader, a station navigator map, a PDA camera that takes and stores photos, a plant analyzer readout and an in-PDA messenger with contacts and group chats.

- **Take** — Each cartridge is an independent, self-contained app you can cherry-pick one at a time, and Crime Assist / SecWatch alone measurably raise the floor for new security players by turning "what do I even charge them with?" into a guided questionnaire.
- **Skip** — Most of the interesting apps are only shells over systems you would have to own first: the bank and stock apps need an economy, MailMetrics needs the mail delivery system, the glimmer monitor needs psionics, so grafting the cartridge without its backing system gives you a screen that reads zero forever.
- **Touches** — security, cargo, service, command, science, all-players
- **Needs** — [player-economy-and-banking](#player-economy-and-banking), [mail-delivery](#mail-delivery), [psionics-and-glimmer](#psionics-and-glimmer), [nanochat-messenger](#nanochat-messenger)
- **Verified** — partly supported. Re-fetching DeltaV's actual pda.yml and cartridges.yml at their vanilla (non-_DV) upstream paths shows the fork edits those upstream-location files directly and extensively: dozens of PDA prototype entries get inline `# DeltaV` cartridge-list edits, and the original WantedListCartridge in the vanilla cartridges.yml is commented out with \"DeltaV: this is replaced by SecWatch\". That's concrete fork-patches-upstream evidence the path-data seed couldn't see. Independently, the claim's own reasoning (needs economy/mail/psionics/messenger backing systems to not be empty shells) is also verified true from the fetched .ftl/.yml content, so integration-would-require-upstream-edits holds too — hence 'both'. The claim's graft_shape of needs-upstream-edits is right, just for a stronger reason (concrete in-place upstream file edits) than it stated.
  - unsupported: "a glimmer monitor charting the station's psionic reading in real time" — evidence only shows a live single-value readout (glimmer-monitor-current-glimmer, Interval, Sync strings); no chart/history string was found (contrast with StockTrading's explicit stock-trading-price-history), so "charting... in real time" implies more visual history than confirmed.
  - unsupported: "an in-PDA messenger with contacts and group chats" attributed to Sunrise — Sunrise's own MessengerCartridge entity description is only "A program for messaging between PDAs"; contacts/group-chat specifics were not found for Sunrise's messenger (that level of detail — CONTACTS, new-chat popups — was only confirmed for DeltaV's separate NanoChat app, not Sunrise's).
  - unsupported: All five Sunrise-specific claims (corporate law reader, navigator map, PDA camera, plant analyzer, messenger) are asserted with zero citations in evidence_cited — I confirmed four of the five app *names* independently via the GitHub API, but the claim's own evidence list does not actually support the Sunrise half of the write-up at all.

### Announcer voice packs

<a id="announcer-voice-packs"></a>

Swappable voiced station announcers covering round start, alert levels, shuttle calls and round-end stingers, plus equipment hailer voice lines.

**corvax, ephemeral-space, frontier, gaby-station-now-dumont-station, goobstation, harmony-station, rmc14, serbia-strong, starlight, sunrise**

*cosmetic · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Instead of the usual text-only bulletins with a generic chime, the station gets a voiced announcer: a real human (or in-character AI) voice reads out round start, alert level changes, and event warnings like meteor showers, ion storms, power outages, outbreaks and alien sightings, and plays a stinger over the round-end screen. Several forks bolt extra flavour onto that, including joke alert levels with their own colour and voice clip, extra named alert states beyond the usual green-to-delta ladder, wall speakers that play announcements spatially so you hear them louder near a speaker and not at all in an unpowered section, and a security hailer button that makes an officer's helmet bark "HALT!", "FREEZE", "GET ON THE FLOOR" out loud to everyone nearby. The lines are typically recorded by a named community voice actor, so the announcer becomes part of the server's identity rather than generic sci-fi filler.

- **Take** — Cheap, high-visibility identity: a recognisable voice on every alert and event is the single most-heard thing on a server, and the bulk of it is audio files plus prototype overrides rather than new mechanics.
- **Skip** — The value is entirely in the recordings, which are the part you cannot copy for free — most packs are one specific voice actor under CC-BY with named attribution, so grafting the plumbing leaves you needing your own voice work, and the wiring still has to reach into upstream event, alert-level and round-end prototypes to swap the sounds.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, command
- **Verified** — partly supported. The claim's needs-upstream-edits downgrade from the drop-in seed is not just correct but understated: Starlight's Resources/Prototypes/AlertLevels/alert_levels.yml (the exact vanilla upstream path, no _Starlight prefix) is directly edited in place, with '# begin starlight'/'# end starlight' comment markers rewriting the sound field of the pre-existing green/blue/violet/yellow/red/gamma/delta/epsilon entries to point at _Starlight audio and appending new omega/theta/psi/orange levels — that is a real fork-patches-upstream file, contradicting the seed's zero-files-outside-namespace premise for this fork. Other forks in the bundle (Goob-Station's Content.Goobstation.Shared/Hailer/HailerComponent.cs, Sunrise's Content.Shared/_Sunrise/AnnouncementSpeaker/Components/AnnouncementSpeakerComponent.cs) are genuinely self-contained new files that would only need upstream wiring (equip slot / helmet-mask prototype, speaker placement) to integrate, matching the second reading. So both readings are simultaneously true across the bundled forks.
  - unsupported: "a security hailer button that makes an officer's helmet bark" — Goob-Station's HailerSystem.cs grants the hail action via GotEquippedEvent gated on SlotFlags.MASK, and the in-character line is sent with nameOverride: Name(uid) + "(SecMask)" — the item is a mask, not a helmet.

## Misc

### Hostile fauna and NPC roster

<a id="hostile-fauna-and-npcs"></a>

Fork-authored creatures and NPC factions with their own sprites, vocalisations and AI behaviour, used as threats, event spawns or pets.

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Players can pick a personal pet in the lobby before the round starts and it spawns next to them at roundstart already bonded to them, with a little control window for ordering it around and a petting interaction that shows hearts. The creature roster itself is much wider than vanilla: dog-eared "inferior vulpkanin" mooks (including syndicate reinforcement versions someone can be dropped in as), a book-shaped library familiar that can be taken over by a ghost, snowmen, elementals, clowns and extra dogs. Most notable is the carp queen, a ghost role shark-queen who eats to fuel summoning a shoal of up to five carp servants and heals herself off the same hunger, so a carp attack can escalate under a real player's direction instead of drifting as dumb mobs. A few pets in the picker are marked sponsor-only, and the same bundle carries linked explosive collars that detonate every collar in a chain when the host wearer dies.

- **Take** — A lobby-picked personal pet gives every player, not just the handful of jobs with a station animal, a small persistent companion to care about, and it is cheap flavour that pays off every single round.
- **Skip** — The spawn path is wired straight into Sunrise's sponsor-validation and player-cache services and a sponsor-only flag on the pet prototype, so grafting it means either importing that donator plumbing or rewriting the gate, and the picker still needs a panel added to the upstream lobby screen plus a component on the player mob prototype.
- **Touches** — all-players, ghost-roles, service, security, antagonist
- **Needs** — [sponsor-and-donator-perks](#sponsor-and-donator-perks), [lobby-and-character-ui](#lobby-and-character-ui)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Adds a grab-bag of new creatures. On the tame end: Largo the cargo gorilla (a ghost can take him over, follow simple orders from supply staff, and be petted), an opossum in tiny protective gear, and a watermelon dog you grow from seeds in botany that then plays as a ghost role loyal to its gardener. On the hostile end: cult-corrupted farm animals that hunt the crew as team antagonists, a Dark Reaper demon summoned from a bloody rune that phases in and out of the material world, screams to stun people, drags victims into itself to heal and grow into larger forms, and floods a room with blood mist. Topping it off is the Ash Drake, an admin-dropped boss that flies across the map leaving a trail of lava, calls down a 20-strong meteor barrage, throws great fireballs, roars, and raises a pack of skeletons to fight alongside it.

- **Take** — It is a ready-made spread of ghost-role content at three difficulty tiers, from a harmless department pet to a full admin-event boss with flight, meteors and summons, so one graft gives both flavour mobs and an event finale.
- **Skip** — Every SS220 file carries an EULA/CLA header with a hosting restriction rather than AGPL, so grafting it into a REUSE-compliant AGPL fork is a licensing problem before it is a code problem; on top of that the corrupted animals are wired into SS220's Cult Yogg faction, telepathy channel and language system and cannot be lifted on their own.
- **Touches** — ghost-roles, antagonist, cargo, botany, all-players
- **Needs** — [blood-cult](#blood-cult), [language-system](#language-system)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**goobstation**

*activity · needs upstream edits · high confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Goob adds a large bestiary on top of the upstream one: asteroid-dwelling fermid bugs that come in small, large, warrior and queen sizes, brullbars and a brullbar king, bears, white wolves, rideable-looking horses, abyss crawlers, gluttons (something that used to be a clown), a "living curse" that shrugs off everything except holy damage, nanite swarms, rogue gunbots and syndicate assault bots, sharkminnows, plus a pile of pets and ghost-role critters (lootbug, homunculus, clown goblin, Trevor, syndicate mothroaches and bees, colour-variant mothroaches, sentient carp and mice). Two behaviours change how fights with them feel: hit one animal in a pack and every friendly creature nearby turns on you at once, and a mob's faction can be swapped on the fly by a status effect so a hostile thing can be temporarily made to stop caring about you. Separately, if a crewmember drifts too far from the station out in space they get a full-screen caution popup and a leviathan roar, and a segmented space whale locks onto them until they get back near the station. There are also worker-bot AI routines: a bot that tends hydroponics trays, one that patrols and welds things shut, and one that refills a machine it is linked to.

- **Take** — It is a big pick-and-choose bestiary you can graft one creature at a time, and the pack-retaliation rule alone makes existing animal encounters read as ecosystems rather than isolated punching bags.
- **Skip** — Every creature drags its own RSI, sounds and ghost-role locale strings, and the roster is heavily Goob-flavoured (goida bot, Trevor, clown goblins) so importing it wholesale imports someone else's server culture along with the monsters; the space whale in particular is a separate opinionated subsystem with its own CVars that punishes leaving the station.
- **Touches** — all-players, salvage, security, service, engineering, ghost-roles, science
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as drop-in, corrected by the verifier. The roster and behaviours are real (I read every cited YAML/C# file and confirmed the mobs, faction-swap status effect, and whale system), but the drop-in shape fails: GroupRetaliationSystem subscribes to NPCRetaliatedEvent, which lives at Content.Server/NPC/Events/NPCRetaliatedEvent.cs marked "goobstation - entire file" (absent from space-wizards master, 404), and NPCRetaliationSystem.cs carries inline "// Goobstation" edits raising that event. The fork both patches upstream directories for this unit and would require the same edits to graft the pack-retaliation mechanic elsewhere.
  - unsupported: "one that patrols and welds things shut" — WeldbotWeldOperator.cs requires a RepairableComponent target and calls RepairableSystem.ApplyRepairs (heals damage; damages targets only when emagged); it is a repair bot, nothing is welded shut
  - unsupported: "they get a full-screen caution popup" — StationProximitySystem.cs uses PopupType.LargeCaution via PopupSystem.PopupEntity, a large popup anchored to the player entity, not a full-screen overlay
  - unsupported: "graft one creature at a time" (take_because) understates per-creature dependencies: the living curse needs Goob's WeakToHoly component plus BiologicalMetaphysical/AdvancedSpirit damage container and modifier set from the religion stack, the space whale needs the MobCaller system and GoobCVars, the sharkminnow needs CollectiveMind

**rmc14**

*activity · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

RMC-14's wildlife and monsters are tuned for a drop-onto-a-hostile-planet round. Simple animals like a small scurrying dog wander the map on their own, while the dangerous creatures start asleep and motionless: they only rouse when a dropship touches down within about thirty tiles of them, so a landing zone that looked quiet suddenly has things waking up around the ramp. Hunting creatures pick their targets with a lot more discrimination than a generic mob would — they preferentially chase people who are still standing, ignore the already-downed, single out someone who can be infected by a facehugger, and steer toward empty resin traps and unfilled egg clusters to stock them, and the leapers close distance with a pounce that needs line of sight, a clear angle and roughly three and a half tiles of range rather than shambling in a straight line. Anyone who dies and turns into a zombie stops being a person on your side entirely: they lose their squad orders and specialist permissions, can no longer be taken over by a ghost or infected further, gain night vision, join a mindless hostile faction, start regenerating heavily (and faster while critical), heal further by biting people, move slightly slower, and their speech is replaced with zombie groaning.

- **Take** — The behaviour pieces solve real problems generic NPCs have: monsters that stay dormant until players actually arrive, hunters that pick sensible targets instead of the nearest body, and a leap attack with proper line-of-sight and angle checks.
- **Skip** — Almost none of it is standalone fauna — the targeting conditions are written against RMC's facehugger infection, resin traps and egg morphers, the wake trigger fires off a dropship-landed-on-planet event, and the zombie pass strips RMC marine order and specialist components, so grafting it means dragging the xenomorph and marine-drop systems along or rewriting every condition.
- **Touches** — all-players, security, antagonist, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive), [shuttle-piloting-extensions](#shuttle-piloting-extensions)
- **Verified** — supported. Every specific mechanical claim checked against the actual source is exact: WakeNPCOnDropshipLandingComponent.Range=30 tiles triggered by DropshipLandedOnPlanetEvent, SleepNPCComponent putting NPCs to sleep on MapInit, NPCLeapComponent.LeapDistance=3.5f/MaxAngleDegrees=5 with a LeapStatus enum literally containing NotInSight/TargetBadAngle/TargetOutOfRange, and RMCZombieSystem.OnZombified removing MarineOrdersComponent/ScoutWhitelistComponent/SniperWhitelistComponent/PyroWhitelistComponent/InfectableComponent/GhostRoleComponent/GhostTakeoverAvailableComponent while adding NightVisionComponent, faction 'RMCDumb', PassiveHealing, PassiveHealingCritMultiplier=1.5f, HealingOnBite, ZombieMovementSpeedDebuff=0.80f, and a ReplacementAccentComponent. Listing the Content.Server/_RMC14/NPC directory also turned up TargetIsStandingCon.cs, TargetIsDownCon.cs, TargetIsNotDeadCon.cs, TargetIsEmptyResinTrapCon.cs and TargetAvailibleEggMorpherCon.cs alongside the cited TargetInfectableCon.cs; these weren't in the evidence_cited list but directly back the standing-vs-downed, resin-trap and egg-cluster targeting claims, so the description is not reaching beyond what the repo contains. On graft_shape: the claim already downgraded the seed's drop-in guess to entangled, and that downgrade is correct. TargetInfectableCon and RMCZombieSystem pull in Content.Shared._RMC14.Xenonids.Parasite (xenomorph-hive) and RMCNPCSystem/WakeNPCOnDropshipLandingComponent pull in Content.Shared._RMC14.Dropship (the dropship/shuttle-piloting system), all while every file itself still lives inside _RMC14 namespace directories, confirming namespace containment does not equal standalone graftability here.

### Food, drink and cooking expansion

<a id="food-and-drink-expansion"></a>

New meals, cocktails, condiments and ingredients with their reactions, cooking recipes and the critics or vendors that consume them.

**rmc14**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Players get sealed military ration packs that they tear open and unpack into a randomised spread of items: one hot entree, a side, a snack, a dessert, a flavoured drink-powder sachet, a bottled water, sometimes a fortune cookie, plus a mini pack of cigarettes and a matchbook, so no two rations are the same and swapping components with squadmates becomes a thing. There are faction-flavoured versions (marine, corporate, and a rival-force canned-food pack) that pull from different item pools. Alongside that comes a bar and mess-hall catalogue: refillable canteens and engraved flasks with adjustable pour sizes that shatter and spill their contents if smashed, named liquor bottles and syrups you can mix drinks from or turn into molotovs, canned sodas, coffee, milk, cups and glasses, and a large microwave cookbook covering pizzas, breads, breakfasts, pastas, desserts, soups, pies, salads and secret recipes.

- **Take** — Enormous ready-made consumables catalogue plus the ration-pack "open it and see what you got" loop, which gives every player a small self-contained thing to do with almost no new mechanics to learn.
- **Skip** — The prototypes are wired into RMC-14's own plumbing (fixed-size/remove-only storage grids, a fork steel material, molotov crafting, and xenomorph interaction blacklists on nearly every item), so every file needs de-linting before it will even load, and the flavour is hard military-sci-fi rather than generic station.
- **Touches** — service, cargo, all-players, security
- **Needs** — [storage-and-inventory-rework](#storage-and-inventory-rework)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**goobstation**

*activity · entangled · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

A large pile of new edibles and smokes: vending-machine junk food (protein bars, lunacake, mochicake, mooncake, tide gobs, jove gello, plutonium rods, venusian hot cakes, oort cloud rocks), mini milk cartons and fitness shakers, tins of red and black caviar you pop open with a pull tab, freedom cheese you slice for burgers, gondola and bingle steaks, strange berries and bluespace tomatoes, and extra burger/skewer fillings so stacked sandwiches show the new ingredients on the sprite. The headline addition is chem-carrying consumables you wear rather than eat: unwrap a lollypop, stick it in your mask slot, and it slowly trickles its 20u solution into you a sip at a time until it is gone (there is a syndicate variant that looks almost identical and is only told apart by its description). Chemlab and blueshield cigarettes and reusable e-cigarettes work the same way for medicine, with a cryostasis version whose pod chemicals refuse to react until smoked.

- **Take** — The wearable-consumable component (lollypops, medicated and cryostasis cigarettes) is a genuinely new way to deliver chemicals over time, and it arrives with a big free pile of vending snacks and drinks that fills out service and cargo variety.
- **Skip** — 826 files of mostly cosmetic snack prototypes means a permanent art, sprite and localisation maintenance tail for content that does not change how a round plays, and several items lean on things you may not have (gondola and bingle meat need those mobs, and the burger-filling entries point at sprites vendored from Einstein Engines and RMC14).
- **Touches** — service, medical, cargo, all-players, antagonist
- **Needs** — [hostile-fauna-and-npcs](#hostile-fauna-and-npcs), [cross-fork-vendored-bundles](#cross-fork-vendored-bundles)
- **Verified** — partly supported; assessed as drop-in, corrected by the verifier. The item inventory and lollypop mechanics are verbatim-accurate (mask slot, 20u maxVol, PassiveConsumable at 0.1u/3s, lookalike syndicate variant, canReact:false cryostasis cig), but cigarettes use the upstream smoking system rather than the new passive mechanic, and drop-in fails in practice: PassiveConsumableComponent.cs imports the fork-only Content.Goobstation.Maths.FixedPoint assembly, bluespace tomato needs Content.Goobstation.Shared/Throwing/SwapTeleportOnThrowComponent, and gondola/bingle meats need Tranquility/BingleJuice reagents from _Goobstation/Reagents/biological.yml - only two of these four cross-system reaches are declared in depends_on.
  - unsupported: "Chemlab and blueshield cigarettes and reusable e-cigarettes work the same way" - none of the cigarette prototypes carry the PassiveConsumable component; cigarette.yml shows ordinary smokables (Cigar/RefillableSolution) that rely on the upstream smoking system, a different mechanism from the lollypop's passive mask-slot drip
  - unsupported: "it slowly trickles its 20u solution into you" - 20u is the solution's maxVol (comment: 'Room for extra chems'); the lollypop as shipped contains only 5u of Sugar, so the 20u payload exists only after someone fills it
  - unsupported: Blueshield cigarettes as medicated smokes - packs.yml only shows CigPackBlueshield inheriting its fill from CigPackSyndicate plus a flavor description mentioning medication; the actual medicated blueshield cigarette contents are not in any cited file

**sunrise**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 9 forks carry it*

Adds a large pile of new things to eat and drink: cocktails like the Negroni, Old Fashioned, Mai Tai, Bramble and Paloma served in their own glasses, Slavic-flavoured drinks like kvass and chamomile tincture, new condiment packets (honey, sour cream), buns, candy, snacks, soups, produce and species-themed dishes such as nian ramen, diona salad and royal xeno soup. Cooking also gets a friendlier machine: instead of setting a timer, the chef loads ingredients, presses one button, and the oven works out which recipe matches and how many portions it can make, complaining out loud if nothing fits. Botany gets a sack of soil that a player can dump on the floor to make a growing plot anywhere on the station, at the cost of some stamina.

- **Take** — It is mostly self-contained content in fork-namespaced folders: dozens of extra meals, drinks and condiments that make the kitchen and bar worth visiting without touching how the round is won or lost.
- **Skip** — Nearly every new drink points at a fork reagent that must be ported alongside it, and the one-button assembler and the soil bag hook into upstream microwave and gathering code, so the "just copy the YAML" part is smaller than the file count suggests.
- **Touches** — service, botany, all-players
- **Needs** — [reagent-and-reaction-expansion](#reagent-and-reaction-expansion)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Custom station events

<a id="custom-station-events"></a>

Additional random midround incidents scheduled against the station: utility ruptures, unknown shuttles, gas leaks, greytide, pest spawns, storms and themed arrivals.

**goobstation**

*structural · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 10 forks carry it*

Instead of a fixed random roll dropping incidents on the station, the round is paced by a hidden director that watches how the shift is actually going (how much fighting, how many injuries, blown-open doors, spilled messes, active anomalies) and then walks the round through a scripted arc such as "quiet, then a mild attack, then power problems, then a repair scramble" or a science-and-engineering-flavoured version, picking which antagonist threat headlines the shift and whether two threats run at once. On top of that scheduling it adds its own incidents: passengers suddenly gain a shared telepathic "Tidemind" chat channel they can talk on, a derelict Syndicate cyborg drifts in from space, Central Command announces an HR distribution error, vent critters crawl out as a joke infestation, a Bingle pit opens for ghosts to jump into late in long rounds, and Christmas content switches itself on seasonally. Ion storms get a much wider pool of AI law rewrites, and admins get canned Central Command announcements for death squads and for CBURN, engineering, security, medical and chaplain response teams, each spelling out for the crew who now outranks whom.

- **Take** — It replaces "random event roulette" with a round that escalates and de-escalates on purpose, so shifts get a shape (calm opening, a spike, a recovery phase) instead of a shuffled list of incidents.
- **Skip** — The director only makes sensible choices if every event prototype in the game carries chaos-cost and event-type tagging, so adopting it means auditing and re-tagging the whole upstream event roster and then re-tuning it after every upstream sync; its antagonist weight table is also written around Goob's own antag roster and needs trimming to whatever your fork actually ships.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, security, engineering, medical, antagonist, ghost-roles, silicon
- **Needs** — [alternate-round-types](#alternate-round-types)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**ephemeral-space**

*structural · entangled · high confidence · 10 forks carry it*

Instead of the game silently rolling the next disaster, the crew gets a timed vote (30-60 seconds) on what goes wrong and where: which department the Gr3y.T1d3 virus bolts open, which APC overloads and catches fire, which gas floods which specific vent, which direction the meteors arrive from, where the critter swarm crawls out. Alongside the familiar breaker flips, power grid checks and vent clogs, the roster adds electrical overloads that start fires and kill every light on a breaker, and a slow "degradation" drip of wear-and-tear: airlocks that jam when you open them, lights that quietly die, mice chewing through maintenance wiring, fake crimes appearing in criminal records, suit sensors going wrong on the crew monitor, radio traffic garbling, emergency access flicking on at random doors, and the next station announcement coming out as distorted prose-poetry. The fork also ends rounds with a radiation storm: escalating countdown announcements from "ETA 30 minutes", the map light shifting to sickly green, space itself becoming lethal to walk into, grid roofs peeling away, and the crew racing to keep the station's thrusters fuelled and the singularity warp drive charged before every organic thing aboard dies.

- **Take** — It converts the event scheduler from a slot machine into something the playerbase actively steers, and the degradation drip gives engineering and security constant low-grade repair work between the big set-piece incidents.
- **Skip** — Nearly every event is wrapped in a fork-wide voting framework that lives outside these files, and the radstorm finale assumes a mobile station with named directional thrusters, a navigation console and a singularity warp drive, so taking the interesting half means dragging in those systems and a matching map too.
- **Touches** — all-players, engineering, security, command, service
- **Needs** — [warp-drive](#warp-drive), [custom-station-maps](#custom-station-maps)
- **Verified** — partly supported. Every cited file fetched cleanly and backs the claim in detail: schedulers.yml + ESEventVoteSchedulerSystem.cs show the crew literally votes on which station event fires next (weighted random pool, 60s vote); the per-event .ftl/yml pairs confirm votes on APC location (electrical overload), gas + vent (gas leak), department (grey tide), meteor direction, and vent-swarm entry point; degradation_events.yml matches every degradation item claimed (airlocks, lights, mice, criminal records, telecoms/radio, crew-monitoring suit sensors, emergency access, distorted-announcement prose-poetry lyrics); round_end.yml + radstorm-rule.ftl confirm the radstorm's map-light color shifts, spaceDangerous flag, removeGridRoof, and directional-thruster/navigation-console/singularity-warp-drive flavor text. Beyond the cited files I fetched Content.Server/_ES/WarpDrive/ESWarpDriveSystem.cs and _ES/Radstorm/ESRadstormModifierMachineSystem.cs, confirming the radstorm's survival mechanic is wired to a separate, large WarpDrive/portal-generator subsystem via a ESThrusterEngineFuelStateChangedEvent and a GetRadstormSpeedMultiplierEvent hook, which corroborates the claim's 'entangled' downgrade (depends_on warp-drive) independent of the seed's namespace-only check. One specific overclaim survives: the claim says the countdown escalates 'from ETA 30 minutes', but ESRadstormRoundEndRuleComponent.cs sets RadstormStartTimeAvg to 72 minutes (Gaussian, stddev 2m) — the first announcement reports a ~72-minute ETA, and 'ETA 30 minutes' is only a later fixed checkpoint (timeBeforeEnd: 30m), not the start of the countdown.
  - unsupported: escalating countdown announcements from "ETA 30 minutes" — the actual first radstorm announcement reports a randomized ETA averaging 72 minutes (RadstormStartTimeAvg = 72m, stddev 2m in ESRadstormRoundEndRuleComponent.cs); 30 minutes is only a mid-sequence checkpoint (timeBeforeEnd: 30m), not the countdown's starting point.

**rmc14**

*structural · entangled · medium confidence · 10 forks carry it*

Mid-round, both sides get a dramatic simultaneous broadcast: High Command tells the marines that an arms-corporation cargo drop went missing on the colony and orders them to recover it, while the Queen Mother warns the hive that a device capable of calling down "skyfire" is sitting near their nest and must not be retrieved. One reinforced AEGIS crate then appears at a random one of several marked spots on the map, along with a random dead body somewhere else that counts as a recoverable intel objective and carries the key. Anyone with the right access can start prying the crate open; after a couple of seconds of it grinding open it disgorges an orbital-bombardment warhead, so the round turns into a race and a firefight over one map object that either faction can end up controlling. Separately, the same bundle adds real-calendar holidays (Christmas, Halloween, New Year, plus fork lore days) that other content can react to, and a set of mapper-placed random spawners so colony ruins, gun caches, loot pools and corpses differ from round to round.

- **Take** — The AEGIS event is a rare thing in SS14: a single midround incident that gives both the crew side and the antagonist side the same physical objective on the map, so the round reorganises itself around one contested location instead of just adding noise.
- **Skip** — It is written for RMC-14's two-faction marine-versus-xeno round and calls straight into that fork's marine and xeno announcement channels, requisitions lift and intel-objective system, so on a normal station fork you would be rewriting the whole payoff (an orbital warhead) and both announcements before a single player sees it; the holidays and spawner components are the only genuinely portable parts.
- **Touches** — command, cargo, security, antagonist, all-players
- **Needs** — [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive), [custom-station-maps](#custom-station-maps)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*activity · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 10 forks carry it*

A grab-bag of extra midround incidents the station has to react to. The gravity generator suddenly loses all charge, so the whole station floats for a couple of minutes after an "attention" announcement before it kicks back on; banana peels rain out of the vents for a minute, and a small fraction of them explode when stepped on; a swarm of mice pours out of the vent spawns with a king rat appearing somewhere on the station; one random mindshielded crewmember's implant starts burning them from the inside. There are also friendly incidents: cargo suddenly gets free gift crates addressed to a department (the kitchen gets meat, dinnerware and a Chefvend restock), and unknown shuttles fly in and dock, including a lost cargo hauler, a travelling Chinese-cuisine restaurant, a disaster escape pod, a clown shuttle, a shuttle full of stranded mimes, and a couple of other fork-specific arrivals.

- **Take** — Cheap, high-variety round texture: most of these are a component plus a YAML entry, and each one gives the crew a fresh problem or a fresh visitor without touching how any department normally plays.
- **Skip** — Every SS220 file carries their own EULA/CLA with a hosting restriction rather than the usual MIT/AGPL, so grafting them into a REUSE-compliant fork is a licensing problem before it is an engineering one; on top of that the unknown-shuttle events are useless without also porting the fork's shuttle grid maps.
- **Touches** — all-players, cargo, engineering, service, security, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [custom-station-maps](#custom-station-maps)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**starlight**

*activity · entangled · medium confidence · 10 forks carry it*

The shift throws far more unscheduled trouble at you than a stock round. Announcements warn that a wreck is about to collide with the hull (small ones just dent it, the "megawreck" one tells you to panic), radiation storms tell everyone to stop spacewalking, an ion cloud dims the sky into a two-minute aurora the crew is invited to go watch through the windows, comms garble into static mid-sentence, doors lock themselves down as a "hostile runtime", the lights drop to night mode, and crawlers get pushed out of the ventilation ducts into occupied rooms. Central Command also issues drills that read as real orders: run a fire or boarding drill, or detain and interview a named crewmate and fax the report back. Separately, unfamiliar shuttles keep drifting into range with ghosts aboard them: a lost cargo hauler, a food truck, a gym, a clown ship, a cruiser, a syndicate escape pod, each arriving with its own announcement or no warning at all. A handheld selector item lets whoever holds it pick an event off a radial dial and fire it, on a cooldown.

- **Take** — It is a big, mostly self-contained pile of midround texture: dozens of announcements, drills, hull collisions and drop-in shuttles that give engineering, security and the ghost pool something to react to on any round type, without touching how the round is won.
- **Skip** — It is shipped as a roster, not a feature: the individual event prototypes reach into Starlight's own antags, species, vent-crawl movement, shuttle maps and their dynamic scheduler's cost/sub-gamemode fields, so taking it wholesale drags in systems you may not want, and taking a subset means auditing each prototype by hand and importing the map files it points at.
- **Touches** — all-players, engineering, security, command, salvage, ghost-roles, atmospherics
- **Needs** — [gamemaster-and-ghost-roles](#gamemaster-and-ghost-roles), [alternate-round-types](#alternate-round-types)
- **Verified** — supported. Fetched all 17 evidence files directly from raw.githubusercontent.com. The locale files confirm the gameplay prose almost verbatim: auroracaelus.ftl gives the two-minute aurora with viewing-port invitation, doorrunetime.ftl gives the "hostile runtime" door lockdown line, radstorm.ftl the spacewalk warning, communicationblackout.ftl the mid-word static garble, nightshift.ftl the dimmed lights, wreck-swarm.ftl the collision/panic announcements including the megawreck Barratry line, and securitydrill.ftl the exact detain-interview-fax-report drill text. unknown_shuttles.yml lists the cargo hauler, food-truck (SRV-Salami), gym, clown ship (honki), cruiser, and syndicate evac pod, each loading its own /Maps/_Starlight/Shuttles/ShuttleEvent/*.yml grid not in evidence_cited — confirming the skip_because point about needing to import map files. events.yml is the strongest confirmation of "entangled": alongside the decorative events it defines full antag round-rule entities (Abductors, Brighteye with speciesHardOverride: Shadekin, several Xenoborg roles, Paradox clones) wired through DynamicRuleCost and SubGamemodes, exactly the "dynamic scheduler's cost/sub-gamemode fields" the claim cites. VentSpawnRule.cs directly imports Content.Shared._Starlight.VentCrawl.EntitySystems/Components, confirming the vent-crawl-movement dependency verbatim. WreckSwarmSystem.cs pulls from the vanilla SalvageMapPrototype pool, backing the "touches: salvage" tag. The claim already downgraded the path-data's drop-in seed to entangled, and every specific reason it gives for that downgrade checks out against the actual file contents, so the downgrade is correct, not an overreach.

### Movement and mobility tweaks

<a id="movement-and-mobility-tweaks"></a>

Small changes to how characters get around: sprinting with a stamina cost, waddling gaits, vent crawling, grappling hooks and carrying small creatures as items.

**rmc14**

*loop-change · entangled · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

A bundle of ways to move yourself, and other people, around a battlefield. You can grab a downed crewmate you are already dragging and hoist them onto your shoulder in a few seconds, then walk (slowly) to safety while they struggle to get free; trained personnel do it faster, and some carriers can even throw the person they are carrying. Elsewhere: ladders let you peek at the deck above or below before committing to the climb, small creatures can slip into an air vent and crawl the duct network to pop out somewhere else on the map (welded vents block them, and examining a vent tells you so), you can jump out of the back of a flying dropship and parachute down, and if you are still standing on a dropship when it leaves you get thrown out and slam into the ground below, taking heavy damage and dragging anyone you were pulling down with you.

- **Take** — Shoulder-carrying a casualty is the single best quality-of-life fix for rescue play — dragging a body across the station is slow, ugly and interruptible, and this replaces it with a readable, contested action anyone can use.
- **Skip** — Only the carry and the ladder peek are really portable; parachuting, crash-landing and vent crawling are built on RMC's dropship, area and xeno framework and would need those concepts invented from scratch, and even the carry is gated on RMC's per-character skill levels.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, security, antagonist, command
- **Needs** — [squads-ranks-and-skills](#squads-ranks-and-skills), [shuttle-piloting-extensions](#shuttle-piloting-extensions), [xenomorph-hive](#xenomorph-hive)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**sunrise**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Three ways to move that the station doesn't normally allow. You can jump: a hop that costs stamina, plays a cartoon boing, and briefly lets you clear low obstacles like tables and railings instead of climbing them, and chaining hops in quick succession builds a small speed boost up to a cap (admins can switch jumping or bunny-hopping off entirely, and players can mute the sound for themselves). You can scoop a person or creature into your arms with a right-click verb if both hands are free: picking up someone conscious and standing takes a while, picking up someone crit or dead is quick, the person being carried can't walk, stand, grab things or be pulled, you move noticeably slower depending on how heavy they are, and you can toss them a short distance. And with the right gear or body, you can spend a couple of seconds squeezing into a floor vent and then travel the station through the ventilation pipes, seeing the pipe network under the floor while you're inside it.

- **Take** — Carrying a crit patient in your arms instead of dragging them face-down across the floor is the single most requested quality-of-life change medics ask for, and it arrives here fully worked out: mass-based slowdown, faster pickup on the unconscious, throw, and every "you can't act while carried" edge case already handled.
- **Skip** — Vent crawling is a stealth-movement channel that quietly rebalances every antagonist and every escape, and jumping over tables undermines the table-as-chokepoint that current combat and chases lean on; taking the bundle whole means re-tuning fights, not just adding a verb.
- **Touches** — all-players, medical, security, antagonist, engineering
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**backmen**

*loop-change · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 7 forks carry it*

Small creatures (and fork monsters like headcrabs and flesh mutants) can climb into an air vent and travel through the station's atmospheric pipe network, popping out of any other vent they reach. While inside you move pipe-tile by pipe-tile with a short delay per step, hear the hiss of gas around you, breathe whatever the pipes are carrying, and can be cooked or crushed by hot or high-pressure lines. Vents welded shut block entry, dead-end pipes stop you, and if an engineer unanchors or destroys a pipe section you get violently ejected. Separately, certain small humanoids can be picked up and stuffed into a backpack or duffel as if they were a bulky item, so someone else can carry them around.

- **Take** — Vent travel gives small antagonists and critters a genuine third movement layer that security cannot simply lock down with doors, and it turns the atmos pipe network from background plumbing into terrain engineers actually have a reason to weld and patrol.
- **Skip** — The crawler only works if the marker components are pinned onto upstream vent and pipe prototypes, and the server system directly references other Backmen/White fork antags (headcrab, flesh), so a graft means editing upstream atmos YAML and unpicking fork-to-fork couplings before it will even compile.
- **Touches** — antagonist, ghost-roles, engineering, atmospherics, security, extra-playable-species
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

### Discord and external service integration

<a id="discord-and-external-services"></a>

Ties the server to the community platform around it: linking a player account to a Discord account and syncing linked roles, gating connection on that link (Discord auth), relaying chat between game and Discord, running in-Discord bot commands against the live server, and pushing station reports, round notifications or rage-quit alerts out over webhooks. Includes standalone bot projects, the EF migrations that store linked ids, and closed-source auth shims. Un-namespaced spellings to sweep for are `MoMMI/` (the relay) and `DiscordAuth/`. Upstream already ships `Content.Server/Discord/` webhooks and a chat link, so only the fork's additions on top of that count.

**rmc14**

*cosmetic · needs upstream edits · medium confidence · 5 forks carry it*

From the lobby a player opens a "Link your Discord account" window, copies a one-time code, clicks a button that opens the server's Discord channel, and pastes the code into a bot prompt to tie the two accounts together. Once linked, supporters get a "Patron Perks" button next to the character preview that opens tabs for writing the lobby message everyone sees on the lobby screen, writing round-end shoutout lines (one for a marine, one for a xeno, 50 characters each) that print in the end-of-round summary, and picking a custom colour for their ghost, plus pointers to named-item and figurine options in character preferences and a credits listing. Separately, staff see it from the other side: messages typed in the Discord admin and mentor channels appear live in their in-game admin chat and mentor chat, with the usual chat ping sound.

- **Take** — It gives a server a real identity loop: players verify against your Discord once, and from then on supporters get visible vanity (lobby message, ghost colour, round-end shoutout) while your admins and mentors can answer tickets from Discord without opening the client.
- **Skip** — None of it changes a single round of play, and the cost is entirely infrastructure you then own forever: a separate Discord bot project in the solution, a bot token and channel config, database columns for linked ids and patron tiers, and patches into the upstream lobby UI. Half the payload (named items, figurines, credits) is only meaningful if you also run the donator-perk content it points at.
- **Touches** — all-players, command, admin, mentor, ghost-roles
- **Needs** — [sponsor-and-donator-perks](#sponsor-and-donator-perks)
- **Verification** — none; read this as a claim, not a finding.

**serbia-strong**

*cosmetic · entangled · high confidence · 5 forks carry it*

Before you can play, the server holds you on a full-screen "link your Discord" panel with a personal link, an "open in browser" button and a quit button; once you finish the link on the community's Discord the panel clears and you drop into the lobby (an admin-enabled "enter without checking" button can skip it). Once linked, the roles you hold in that Discord follow you into the game as a supporter tier, which is what grants things like priority when the server is full. Outside the game, the community Discord channel gets pinged with round-start, round-end and round-restart posts naming the server and map, ban notices show up there too, and staff can query player count/server status or fire console commands at the live server from their own tooling.

- **Take** — It is the standard lever for tying a public server to its community: a hard Discord-link gate cuts drive-by griefers and ban evaders, and the same link doubles as the identity backbone for supporter perks and priority join.
- **Skip** — The SS220 files carry a custom EULA/CLA with a hosting restriction rather than the usual MIT/AGPL, they only work against a closed backend API the fork hosts (link URL, API key, sponsor roster endpoint), and the connection gate is welded to their sponsor-tier system, so porting means writing your own web service and licence-scrubbing anyway.
- **Touches** — all-players, command
- **Needs** — [sponsor-and-donator-perks](#sponsor-and-donator-perks), [role-slots-and-join-queue](#role-slots-and-join-queue)
- **Verified** — supported. Every concrete beat of the gameplay description checks out in code. DiscordLinkRequiredState is a Robust client State (full-screen, replaces the active UI root) hosting DiscordLinkRequiredGui, which has QuitButton, OpenUrlButton (opens UrlEdit's AuthUrl), and a ByPassCheck button gated visible only by CCVars220.ByPassDiscordLinkCheck (admin-set cvar) — matches the link-gate/quit/browser-link/admin-bypass description exactly. DiscordPlayerManager.cs defines SponsorTier.PriorityJoinTiers and hits `{linkApiUrl}/api/userinfo/sponsors` (sponsor roster endpoint) and `/api/linkAccount/*` (link URL) with a Bearer API key from CCVars220.DiscordLinkApiKey — confirms supporter-tier-from-Discord-roles and closed-backend dependency. Content.Server/Corvax/JoinQueue/JoinQueueManager.cs directly calls `_discordPlayerManager.HasPriorityJoinTier`/`HaveFreeSponsorSlot` to sort/bypass the join queue — this is real cross-namespace (Corvax vs SS220) coupling to a separate system, confirming the claim's own downgrade to 'entangled' and its depends_on list (sponsor-and-donator-perks, role-slots-and-join-queue) rather than contradicting it. RoundNotificationsSystem.cs posts round-start/end/restart Discord embeds naming server and map exactly as described. ServerControlController.cs exposes /console-command, /players, /server-status behind a WatchdogToken header, matching 'staff can query player count/server status or fire console commands.' Ban notices are real too: Content.Server/SS220/Discord/DiscordBanPostManager.cs (not in the cited evidence list, found by browsing the SS220/Discord folder) posts ban info to the same closed backend API. CLA.txt confirms the custom hosting-restricted license cited in skip_because. The claim already self-downgraded the seed's 'drop-in' to 'entangled', and my own reading independently confirms real entanglement (JoinQueueManager depending on DiscordPlayerManager), so the claim's graft_shape call is correct, not an overclaim.

### Sponsor and donator perks

<a id="sponsor-and-donator-perks"></a>

Out-of-round player entitlements granted by tier rather than earned in the round: a sponsor or donator manager backed by an external sponsor API or a local tier list, and the perks it unlocks such as sponsor-only loadout entries and clothing, custom ghost sprites and titles, extra character slots, priority placement in the join queue, and the meta-currency or premium-pass storefront that sells them. The queue and slot machinery itself stays in `role-slots-and-join-queue`; in-character money stays in `player-economy-and-banking`; the account link that identifies the sponsor stays in `discord-and-external-services`.

### Station goals

<a id="station-goals"></a>

A round-start, station-wide objective drawn from a prototype pool and delivered to the crew as a printed paper or fax announcement, with a CVar to enable it and an admin command to re-send it. Corvax-lineage and widely inherited. It is usually invisible to namespace-driven discovery because forks add it to un-namespaced `Content.Server/StationGoal/`, `Content.Shared/StationGoal/`, `Content.Shared/CCVar/CCVars.StationGoals.cs` and `Resources/Locale/en-US/station-goal/` rather than under a fork prefix.
